> DEVELOPER

Work on Linear issue ABA-299:

<issue identifier="ABA-299">
<title>Resume a halted issue on re-run (reuse the preserved worktree)</title>
<description>
## What

On re-run after a halt, drain-cycle will **reuse the preserved worktree** instead of failing on the existing branch, and spawn a **fresh** `claude` session whose prompt says "prior work is committed on this branch — review it and continue from where it left off." A fresh session means a **clean context, so the per-issue token budget resets** (the whole reason the operator raised the cap). On Done, the existing teardown removes the worktree as normal.

**Scope.** The change is deliberately narrow: automatic only for the clean post-halt state (registered worktree + branch); bounded by a resume-attempt cap; preserves a dirty tree as-is (never resets/cleans); not in-process auto-retry. The manual escape hatch (delete worktree + branch by hand) remains the way to force a fresh start.

## Why

### The mechanics today

`drain-cycle` spawns one `claude -p` worker per Linear issue in an isolated git worktree at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<id>` on a branch named `<id>`. When a worker is SIGKILLed mid-issue (the dominant case: a per-issue **token-cap breach**), the orchestrator reverts the issue's Linear state to its pre-run value (e.g. `Todo`, so it is re-picked next invocation) but **leaves the worktree and branch in place** for inspection (`docs/design-decisions.md` §3).

### The gap

On re-run, the issue is re-picked, but `worktree.add` runs `git worktree add -b <id> …`, which fails because that branch already exists. Today this surfaces as a clean `Halt: … — setup failed: … a branch named '<id>' already exists` (the generic pre-spawn handler already turns the `RuntimeError` into spec-shaped UX — so the "detect cleanly" goal of the superseded <issue id="3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b">ABA-231</issue> is effectively met). But the run cannot proceed: the operator must manually delete the worktree/branch, **discarding any committed work the killed session produced**.

### Motivating case

A token-breached run on <issue id="f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c">ABA-276</issue> had already committed 2 commits / 142 lines to the `ABA-276` branch before being killed. That work was the durable record of progress — and the only way forward was to throw it away and re-drain from scratch (re-spending \~8M tokens).

### The design decision this reverses

`docs/design-decisions.md` §8 currently pins: *"Retry. A halted issue is not retried automatically. The operator inspects the worktree and decides — fix, redo manually, or descope."* This work **partially reverses** that and so must add a new **§14** that supersedes the §8 "Retry" bullet (per the "memory lives in artefacts / ADRs are superseded, not silently ignored" rule). (§13 is already taken by "Opt-in OpenTelemetry tracing to Honeycomb", which landed after this issue was shaped — the next free section number is §14; the agent should confirm the highest section at implementation time.) The reversal is narrow and must be documented as such:

* Resume reuses a **preserved, committed** work-in-progress on a **manual re-run** (or scripted re-run). It is **not** in-process auto-retry, and it is **bounded** by a resume-attempt cap (below).
* §3 (worktree preservation on halt) and §10 (`link_project_config`) are **relied upon**, not changed.
* The `docs/tasks/rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md` framing ("no auto-cleanup, operator owns retry; detect-and-halt only") is superseded by this work; note it in §14. (The corresponding Linear issue, <issue id="3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b">ABA-231</issue>, is already Canceled/archived — this issue replaces its stance.)

### Why not `claude --resume`

A SIGKILLed worker has `session_id: null` in the run log (it dies before the terminal `result` event), so `--resume` isn't even available. And `--resume` would reload the exact context that blew the token budget, re-breaching almost immediately. The committed branch — not the session — is the correct resume point.

### Assumptions (the core bet)

* **A1.** The dominant halt is a token-cap breach, and a killed worker has usually committed incremental work to the `<id>` branch (true in the <issue id="f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c">ABA-276</issue> case: 2 commits). If a worker dies before committing anything, resume re-runs from an empty branch — no worse than today, and bounded by the cap (A3).
* **A2.** Only the clean post-halt state (registered worktree + branch) is worth auto-handling. Operator-induced partial states (branch without worktree, orphan dir) are rare and acceptably handled by the **existing** clean halt — no new code. This is the main simplification; if partial-state auto-recovery turns out to be wanted, it's an additive follow-up.
* **A3.** A fresh session, pointed at committed work and told to continue, will build on it rather than restart. This is the core bet; the resume prompt directive (`git log`/`git status` first) is the cheap insurance, and the resume-attempt cap is the backstop if the bet fails for a given issue.

## How

### Resolved design choices

* **Resume is automatic on re-run** (the operator's explicit intent). Forcing a fresh start is the manual escape hatch: delete the worktree + branch (`git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`), exactly as we just did for <issue id="f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c">ABA-276</issue>. No new flag needed.
* **Resume-attempt cap = 3** (configurable). After 3 non-Done attempts of an issue within the cycle, drain-cycle halts **before spawning** (zero token burn) and leaves the issue for manual intervention. This is the only backstop: the cycle-wide token cap does **not** fire on repeated halts — the breach path `return 1`s before `check_cycle`, and each re-run is a new process with a fresh `RunLog`, so cumulative cycle tokens reset every invocation.
* **Dirty preserved tree: preserve as-is.** The fresh session reconciles uncommitted edits via `git status`/`git log`. Never `reset --hard`/`clean` (would destroy the saved work and the gitignored config symlinks). Never switch teardown to `--force`.

### Worktree reuse logic (two branches, not five)

For picked issue `<id>` in `target_repo`, with `path = target_repo/.worktrees/<id>`:

1. **A worktree is already registered at** `path` → reuse it. Run **no git command** — just return `(path, resumed=True)`. This is the clean post-halt state (the <issue id="f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c">ABA-276</issue> case) and the only state worth auto-handling.
2. **Otherwise** → call today's `add` (`git worktree add -b <id> <path> main`), returning `(path, resumed=False)`.

That's it. The rare leftover states — branch exists but no worktree, or an orphan directory at `path` — make `add` raise `RuntimeError` (with git's stderr), which the orchestrator's **existing** pre-spawn handler already turns into the clean `Halt: … — setup failed: …` line. We do **not** add bespoke detection, `prune`, branch re-checkout, or a new exception type for those: they're operator-induced, rare, and the operator clears the leftover by hand (`git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`). **The detection is one git call** (`git worktree list --porcelain`, parsed for a record at `path.resolve()`) plus the existing `add`.

Verified against real git: any `git worktree add` onto an already-registered path fails, so the reuse case must **not** call `add` — hence the registration check first. Reuse is a pure no-op on git state, so a dirty index / staged / untracked files in the preserved worktree survive untouched (this is the "preserve as-is" choice, for free).

### Component changes

#### 1\. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new `ensure` (keep `add` as-is)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class WorktreeHandle:
    path: Path
    resumed: bool

def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> WorktreeHandle:
    """Reuse a preserved worktree if one is registered at the expected
    path, else create a fresh one via add(). Reuse runs no git command."""
    path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
    if _is_registered_worktree(repo, path):
        return WorktreeHandle(path, resumed=True)
    return WorktreeHandle(add(repo, identifier), resumed=False)
```

* One private helper: `_is_registered_worktree(repo, path) -> bool` — runs `git worktree list --porcelain` (via the existing `_run_git`-style call) and checks for a `worktree` record equal to `path.resolve()`. No `WorktreeStateError`, no `prune`, no branch re-checkout, no `_branch_exists`.
* **Keep** `add(repo, identifier) -> Path` **exactly as-is** — `tests/test_worktree.py::test_add_failure_raises_with_git_stderr_in_message` pins it as the fail-on-duplicate primitive that `ensure` reuses for the fresh/leftover path, and its `RuntimeError` is what the orchestrator's existing handler turns into the clean halt.
* `link_project_config` is already idempotent (`os.path.lexists` skip); call it unconditionally after `ensure` for both fresh and resumed (confirmed safe — returns `[]` on reuse).

#### 2\. `drain_cycle/prompt.py` — `resumed` kwarg

```python
def build(issue: dict[str, Any], worktree: Path, *, resumed: bool = False) -> str: ...
```

* When `resumed=True`, prepend a resume segment as the **first line of the preamble** (after the `---` separator, before "Execution instructions:") so it leads the instructions but **does not displace** `_TAIL` **from last position** (the four-segment ordering is load-bearing). Text directs the agent to run `git log --oneline main..HEAD` and `git status` first and continue rather than restart.
* `resumed=False` default keeps every current call site and all existing `tests/test_prompt.py` assertions unchanged.

#### 3\. `drain_cycle/limits.py` — `max_resume_attempts`

* Add `max_resume_attempts: int | None = 3` to the `Limits` dataclass. It is a **count**, not a resource ceiling, but lives here because `limits.yml` is the natural config home; `null` disables. The existing `_coerce`/`load` path validates it (positive number or null; rejects bool/non-positive) with no change.

#### 4\. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — swap `add`→`ensure`, thread flag, add cap guard

In the pre-spawn try-block (`orchestrator.py:169-205`), replace `worktree.add(...)` with `worktree.ensure(...)` and insert the cap check **before** `linear.set_state(..., "In Progress")` (no spawn ⇒ no Linear transition, mirroring the resolution-halt contract):

```python
handle = worktree.ensure(target_repo, identifier)   # RuntimeError on leftover/conflict → existing halt
worktree_path = handle.path
worktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths)
if handle.resumed and limits.max_resume_attempts is not None \
        and _resume_attempts(log.cycle_id, identifier) >= limits.max_resume_attempts:
    # clean halt BEFORE set_state: run-log entry (cached issue state), stderr Halt line, return 1.
    # No revert (state was never moved). Distinct reason names the cap.
    ...
linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
...
agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path, resumed=handle.resumed)
```

* New `_resume_attempts(cycle_id, identifier) -> int`: glob `runlog.runs_dir()/<cycle_id>-*.json` (reusing the per-cycle filename convention) and count entries with `issue_identifier == identifier` and `final_linear_state != "Done"` (prior non-Done attempts). The current run's just-created empty file contributes nothing.
* The `resumed` flag flows **only** to `prompt.build`, never to `worker.run_issue` — the worker always spawns a fresh session (`session_id` is null on kill; `--resume` is unavailable and undesirable).
* Cap-halt reuses the `_halt_message` helper with an appended reason, so the stderr line and the run-log `halt_reason` stay single-sourced.

#### 5\. Docs

* `docs/design-decisions.md`: add **§14** (resume on re-run) documenting the reversal of the §8 Retry bullet, the state matrix, the resume cap, the dirty-tree choice, and why `--resume` is not used. Annotate the §8 Retry bullet as superseded by §14. (Confirm the next free section number at implementation time — §13 is OpenTelemetry tracing.)
* `README.md`: the halt example currently says the worktree is "left in place"; add that a re-run now **resumes** it, and document `max_resume_attempts` alongside the other `limits.yml` keys.
* `docs/tasks/rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md`: note it is superseded by this feature.

### Test plan

(Mirrors existing patterns: real git in tmp repos; monkeypatched Linear + fake-claude shell script; no conftest.) `pytest` **green is the per-PR-layer precondition** (see Delivery) — the unit and integration tests below are the layer-by-layer gate, distinct from the behavioural Acceptance criteria.

`tests/test_worktree.py` (real git, existing `_init_repo`):

* fresh → `WorktreeHandle(resumed=False)`, worktree+branch created.
* reuse registered worktree → call `ensure` once, commit + stage + leave untracked files, call `ensure` again → same path, `resumed=True`, and `git status --porcelain` / `git log` unchanged (pins the no-op-on-git guarantee).
* leftover branch without worktree (operator removed the worktree) → `ensure` falls through to `add`, which raises `RuntimeError` (confirms leftover states hit the existing halt path, not a crash).
* `link_project_config` idempotent on reuse → returns `[]`, symlink intact.

`tests/test_orchestrator_resume.py` (new; mirror `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`):

* **headline**: pre-seed worktree+branch+commit for the first issue, stub it `Todo`, fake-claude marks it Done → exit 0, **no worktree-add failure**, worktree removed on Done, entry `final_linear_state == "Done"`.
* resumed prompt mentions prior work: prompt-capturing fake-claude on a pre-seeded worktree → spawned prompt contains the resume directive and `<id>`.
* resume cap: pre-seed 3 prior non-Done entries for `<id>` in `runs/<cycle-id>-*.json`, `max_resume_attempts=3` → exit 1 with the cap halt reason, `set_state` not called, no spawn.

`tests/test_prompt.py`: `resumed=True` includes the resume directive + identifier and `_TAIL` is still the last non-empty line; `resumed=False` is byte-identical to today's prompt.

`tests/test_limits.py`: `max_resume_attempts` honours absent-key default (3), explicit `null` (off), positive override, and rejects bool/non-positive via `_coerce`.

### Task breakdown

1. `worktree.py`: `WorktreeHandle`, `_is_registered_worktree`, `ensure`. `add` untouched.
2. `tests/test_worktree.py`: reuse + leftover-falls-through tests — get git mechanics green first.
3. `prompt.py` + `tests/test_prompt.py`: resume variant.
4. `limits.py` + `tests/test_limits.py`: `max_resume_attempts`.
5. `orchestrator.py`: swap `add`→`ensure`, thread `resumed`, add `_resume_attempts` + cap guard.
6. `tests/test_orchestrator_resume.py`: integration tests.
7. `docs/design-decisions.md` §14, `README.md`, `rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md` note.

## Delivery — author as a 4-PR Graphite stack

**This issue is not drained via drain-cycle and is not split into Linear sub-issues.** It is authored by **one supervised agent in a single session**, working on a branch off `main` in the repo root (not a `.worktrees/` sandbox), and delivered as a **Graphite (**`gt`**) PR stack** — one PR per layer, reviewed bottom→top. This is the owner-authorised exception to the AGENTS.md "push directly to main; PRs only when the owner asks" default. <issue id="21915a3a-c443-4c9e-a032-b0964f0ed845">ABA-299</issue> stays the single tracked issue.

**Precondition:** `gt` (1.8.x) authenticated against `origin` (`github.com/ababushkin/drain-cycle`); the session is supervised, so branch/PR creation runs with approvals. The repo has **no CI**, so each layer's gate is a local `pytest` run — not a remote check.

**The stack (maps the 7 tasks above onto 4 PRs):**

| PR | Layer | Tasks | Contents | Green on its own |
| -- | -- | -- | -- | -- |
| PR1 | worktree primitive | 1, 2 | `WorktreeHandle`, `_is_registered_worktree`, `ensure`; `test_worktree.py` reuse/leftover tests. `add` untouched. | yes (its unit tests) |
| PR2 | prompt variant | 3 | `prompt.build(resumed=)` + resume segment; `test_prompt.py`. | yes |
| PR3 | limits knob | 4 | `max_resume_attempts` on `Limits`; `test_limits.py`. | yes |
| PR4 | wiring + docs | 5, 6, 7 | orchestrator `add`→`ensure`, thread `resumed`, `_resume_attempts` + cap guard; `test_orchestrator_resume.py`; **and docs (§14, README, supersession note) in this same PR**. | yes (full suite) |

PR1–3 are independent leaves (different files), each green on its own even though no production caller uses them yet. PR4 is the only behaviour-changing layer and integrates the three below it. **Docs land in PR4** so the §8-reversal ADR ships in the same PR as the behaviour that reverses it (an architectural change updates its ADR in the same PR — don't trail the docs in a later PR).

**Authoring loop — one layer at a time, gated:**

1. `gt create` a branch for the layer (conventional-commit subject, e.g. `feat: worktree.ensure reuses a preserved worktree`).
2. Run `pytest` — **the layer's own tests must be green** before submitting (no CI; local pytest is the only gate).
3. `gt submit` — opens the real GitHub PR for that layer on `origin`.
4. **STOP and report the PR; wait for the operator's go-ahead.** Do **not** start the next layer until approved.
5. On approval, build the next layer **on top** with `gt create` (never branch from `main` for layers 2–4 — the stack is one lineage; `gt` sets the parent).
6. On review feedback for a lower layer: `gt down`, amend, `gt restack`, `gt submit` to update the stack. The same agent restacks the whole tower — it has full context, which is the reason one agent authors all four layers rather than handing each to a fresh worker.

**Completion ritual is adapted for the stack:** `/code-review-and-quality` + commit happen **per layer** (before each `gt submit`); the Linear issue moves to **Done once the whole stack is approved and merged**, not per layer. Do not push individual layers straight to `main`.

## Acceptance criteria

Observable end-to-end checks, verified after the stack merges. (`pytest` green is the per-PR-layer precondition — see Test plan — not a behavioural criterion.)

* **Headline resume.** In a throwaway repo, `worktree.add` an issue branch, commit a file, leave the worktree; run the orchestrator (with a fake/cheap model) against a stubbed cycle containing that issue → it resumes (prompt mentions prior work), completes, and the worktree is removed on Done.
* **Token budget resets.** The resumed run's log entry has a non-null, distinct `session_id` and `cumulative_tokens` reflecting only this session's spend — not the killed run's.
* **Manual escape hatch.** After `git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`, the next drain creates a fresh worktree (`resumed=False`) and runs normally.
* **Dirty preserved tree.** With staged + untracked edits left in the preserved worktree, drain runs and the resumed session sees them via `git status` — no `reset`/`clean` ran; the symlinked config dirs survive.
* **Leftover partial state.** With the worktree directory deleted but the branch kept, drain halts cleanly with `Halt: … — setup failed: …` (no traceback, no Linear transition).
* **Resume cap fires.** Seed 3 non-Done run-log entries for an id; the next run halts before spawn with the cap reason, `set_state` not called.
* **Cap disable.** With `max_resume_attempts: null` in `limits.yml` and ≥3 seeded non-Done entries, drain proceeds rather than halting.

## Out of scope

(Deliberate, v2 candidates.)

* **Auto-recovery of operator-induced partial states** (branch without worktree → re-checkout; orphan/prunable dir → prune). Deliberately cut for simplicity (A2): these hit the existing clean halt and the operator clears the leftover by hand. Add only if it proves needed.
* Per-resume token budget (a tighter cap for resumed sessions) — the count cap is the load-bearing guard for v1.
* Skipping a capped issue to drain the rest of the cycle (today any halt stops the run; a capped issue blocks the cycle until the operator intervenes — consistent with the existing halt contract).
* A `drain-cycle clean <id>` recovery subcommand.
* `claude --resume` session continuation (unavailable on kill; undesirable for token-breach).

## Supersedes

Replaces the "operator owns retry / detect-and-halt only" stance of <issue id="3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b">ABA-231</issue> (already Canceled). This issue is the resume-enabled successor: it reuses the preserved worktree rather than just halting cleanly on re-run.

7	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
8	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
9	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,

12	from __future__ import annotations

14	import os

30	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier

44	) -> list[Path]:
45	    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the worktree.

53	
54	    For each name: skip it if absent in ``repo`` (a clean no-op for repos
55	    without that config) or if something already occupies that path in the
56	    worktree (a tracked entry git checked out, or a pre-existing link). The
57	    check uses ``os.path.lexists`` so a dangling link counts as present and is
58	    never clobbered. Returns the links created.
59	    """
60	    created: list[Path] = []
61	    repo = repo.resolve()
62	    for name in names:
63	        source = repo / name
64	        if not source.exists():
65	            continue
66	        link = worktree_path / name
67	        if os.path.lexists(link):
68	            continue
69	        os.symlink(source, link)
70	        created.append(link)
71	    return created
72	
73	
74	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
75	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
76	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.remove") as span:
77	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
78	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
79	        _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
80	
81	
82	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
83	    result = subprocess.run(
84	        ["git", *args],
85	        cwd=cwd,
86	        check=False,
87	        capture_output=True,
88	        text=True,
89	    )
90	    if result.returncode != 0:
91	        raise RuntimeError(
92	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
93	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
94	        )
95	
```

1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	
16	_TAIL = (
17	    "before marking Done: run /code-review-and-quality on the working-tree "
18	    "changes, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
19	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
20	)
21	
22	
23	def build(issue: dict[str, Any], worktree: Path) -> str:
24	    title = issue.get("title", "")
25	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
26	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
27	
28	    preamble = (
29	        "---\n\n"
30	        "Execution instructions:\n"
31	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
32	        "- Base branch: main\n"
33	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
34	        "before marking Done):\n"
35	        "  1. Run `/code-review-and-quality` against the working-tree changes.\n"
36	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
37	        "are at your discretion.\n"
38	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
39	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
40	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
41	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
42	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
43	        '(state: "Done").\n'
44	    )
45	
46	    return (
47	        f"# {title}\n\n"
48	        f"{description}\n\n"
49	        f"{preamble}\n"
50	        f"{_TAIL}\n"
51	    )
52

1	"""Per-issue and per-cycle resource guardrails.
2	
3	A spawned ``claude -p`` worker can run away — burning tokens, wall-clock,
4	and quota with no ceiling but the outer per-issue timeout. This module
5	defines the thresholds that bound that spend and the ``Breach`` value that
6	names which one was crossed.
7	
8	**Two layers, belt and suspenders.**
9	
10	* *Native belt* — ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to ``claude`` as
11	  ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost without the
12	  orchestrator having to watch it.
13	* *Orchestrator suspenders* — ``per_issue_tokens`` and ``per_issue_seconds``
14	  are enforced by the worker against the live event stream: when either is
15	  crossed the worker SIGKILLs the session's process group (see
16	  ``worker.py``). The ``cycle_*`` caps are enforced by the orchestrator
17	  between issues via ``check_cycle`` — death-by-aggregate, where every issue
18	  is individually under its cap but their sum drains the quota, is exactly
19	  what the per-issue caps cannot catch.
20	
21	Token count is the primary guardrail (a subscription user pays in tokens,
22	not dollars); cost rides alongside it.
23	
24	**On/off per guardrail.** Any threshold may be ``None`` — that guardrail is
25	off. The defaults below are all live; an operator turns one off by setting
26	its key to ``null`` in ``~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml``.
27	
28	**Load precedence.** Baked-in defaults, overlaid by ``limits.yml`` if it
29	exists: a key absent from the file keeps its default, an explicit ``null``
30	turns the guardrail off, a positive number overrides it. A present-but-
31	malformed file raises ``LimitsConfigError`` rather than silently falling
32	back to defaults — a typo that quietly disabled a cap the operator believed
33	was active is worse than a loud startup halt. The defaults are deliberately
34	generous starting points; recalibrate against real run-log spend.
35	"""
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
39	from pathlib import Path
40	from typing import Any
41	
42	import yaml
43	
44	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml"
45	
46	
47	class LimitsConfigError(RuntimeError):
48	    """``limits.yml`` exists but is malformed."""
49	
50	
51	@dataclass(frozen=True)
52	class Breach:
53	    """A crossed guardrail, recorded into the halting run-log entry.
54	
55	    ``scope`` is ``"per-issue"`` or ``"cycle"``; ``metric`` is ``"token"``,
56	    ``"time"``, or ``"cost"``. ``limit`` is the cap that was set and
57	    ``observed`` the value at the moment of the breach. ``describe`` renders
58	    the operator-facing line — the worker uses it for a per-issue kill and
59	    the orchestrator for a cycle stop, so the wording cannot drift between
60	    the two surfaces.
61	    """
62	
63	    scope: str
64	    metric: str
65	    limit: float
66	    observed: float
67	
68	    def describe(self) -> str:
69	        return f"{self.scope} {self.metric} cap exceeded: {self._detail()}"
70	
71	    def _detail(self) -> str:
72	        if self.metric == "cost":
73	            return f"${self.observed:,.2f} ≥ ${self.limit:,.2f}"
74	        if self.metric == "time":
75	            return f"{self.observed:,.0f}s ≥ {self.limit:,.0f}s"
76	        return f"{self.observed:,.0f} ≥ {self.limit:,.0f} tokens"
77	
78	
79	@dataclass(frozen=True)
80	class Limits:
81	    """Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.
82	
83	    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·
84	    90 min · $60.
85	    """
86	
87	    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000
88	    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60
89	    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0
90	    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000
91	    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60
92	    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0
93	
94	
95	def default_config_path() -> Path:
96	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
97	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "limits.yml"
98	
99	
100	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Limits:
101	    """Read ``limits.yml`` over the baked-in defaults.
102	
103	    A missing or empty file yields the defaults unchanged — ``limits.yml``
104	    is optional. A present file maps limit names to positive numbers (or
105	    ``null`` to disable that guardrail); unknown keys, non-numeric values,
106	    booleans, and non-positive numbers each raise ``LimitsConfigError`` with
107	    a message naming the on-disk problem.
108	    """
109	    defaults = Limits()
110	    if path is None:
111	        path = default_config_path()
112	    if not path.exists():
113	        return defaults
114	    try:
115	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
116	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
117	        raise LimitsConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}") from None
118	    if data is None:
119	        return defaults
120	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
121	        raise LimitsConfigError(
122	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must be a mapping of limit names to numbers"
123	        )
124	
125	    valid = [f.name for f in fields(Limits)]
126	    unknown = set(data) - set(valid)
127	    if unknown:
128	        raise LimitsConfigError(
129	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} has unknown keys: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}; "
130	            f"valid keys: {', '.join(valid)}"
131	        )
132	
133	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
134	    for name in valid:
135	        if name not in data:
136	            values[name] = getattr(defaults, name)
137	            continue
138	        values[name] = _coerce(name, data[name])
139	    return Limits(**values)
140	
141	
142	def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:
143	    """Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number."""
144	    if raw is None:
145	        return None
146	    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would
147	    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.
148	    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:
149	        raise LimitsConfigError(
150	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number "
151	            f"or null (got {raw!r})"
152	        )
153	    return raw
154	
155	
156	def check_cycle(
157	    limits: Limits, *, tokens: int, cost_usd: float, seconds: float
158	) -> Breach | None:
159	    """Return the first cycle-wide cap the running totals breach, else ``None``.
160	
161	    Tokens are checked first (the primary guardrail), then cost, then
162	    wall-clock. A guardrail set to ``None`` is skipped.
163	    """
164	    if limits.cycle_tokens is not None and tokens >= limits.cycle_tokens:
165	        return Breach("cycle", "token", limits.cycle_tokens, tokens)
166	    if limits.cycle_cost_usd is not None and cost_usd >= limits.cycle_cost_usd:
167	        return Breach("cycle", "cost", limits.cycle_cost_usd, cost_usd)
168	    if limits.cycle_seconds is not None and seconds >= limits.cycle_seconds:
169	        return Breach("cycle", "time", limits.cycle_seconds, seconds)
170	    return None
171	
```

1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import os
12	import sys
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	from opentelemetry.trace import Span
17	
18	from . import linear, model, progress, prompt, runlog, telemetry, worker, worktree
19	from .limits import Limits, check_cycle
20	from .linear import DependencyCycleError
21	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
22	
23	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
24	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
25	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
26	_DEBUG_ENV_VAR = "DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG"
27	"""Opt-in switch for per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture. Any non-empty value
28	turns it on; the worker then writes each session's startup diagnostics
29	(settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, hooks) beside the run log. Off by
30	default — the diagnostic exists for one-shot investigation, not steady state.
31	See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10."""
32	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
33	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
34	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
35	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
36	rather than a misleading fake path."""
37	
38	
39	def _now_iso() -> str:
40	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
41	
42	
43	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
44	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
45	
46	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
47	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
48	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
49	    time.
50	    """
51	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
52	
53	
54	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
55	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
56	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
57	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
58	
59	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
60	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
61	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
62	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
63	
64	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
65	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
66	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
67	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
68	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
69	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
70	    """
71	    try:
72	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
73	    except Exception as exc:
74	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
75	    try:
76	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
77	    except Exception:
78	        return target_state_name, None
79	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
80	
81	
82	def _worker_log_fields(result: worker.WorkerResult) -> dict[str, object]:
83	    """Map a ``WorkerResult`` onto the run-log entry's usage fields.
84	
85	    Shared by all three worker-backed ``append_entry`` calls (timeout
86	    halt, Done, not-Done halt) so the recorded usage shape can't drift
87	    between branches.
88	    """
89	    return {
90	        "duration_seconds": result.duration_seconds,
91	        "model": result.model,
92	        "usage": result.usage,
93	        "cost_usd": result.cost_usd,
94	        "num_turns": result.num_turns,
95	        "session_id": result.session_id,
96	        "is_error": result.is_error,
97	    }
98	
99	
100	def _debug_enabled() -> bool:
101	    """Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.
102	
103	    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any
104	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator
105	    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1
106	    drain-cycle`` and leaves it off otherwise.
107	    """
108	    return bool(os.environ.get(_DEBUG_ENV_VAR))
109	
110	
111	def run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits | None = None) -> int:
112	    """Drain the current cycle inside the ``drain.cycle`` root span.
113	
114	    The span wrapper is thin so the body keeps its shape; per-issue work nests
115	    under it via ``_drain_one_issue``'s ``drain.issue`` spans, and the Linear,
116	    worktree, and worker spans nest under those — yielding one trace per drain.
117	    """
118	    if limits is None:
119	        limits = Limits()
120	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.cycle") as cycle_span:
121	        return _run(repos, limits, cycle_span)
122	
123	
124	def _run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits, cycle_span: Span) -> int:
125	    debug = _debug_enabled()
126	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
127	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.cycle_id", cycle_id)
128	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
129	    try:
130	        plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
131	    except DependencyCycleError as exc:
132	        halt_reason = f"Halt: {exc}"
133	        log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
134	        telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-dependency-cycle", halt_reason)
135	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
136	        return 1
137	
138	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_planned", len(plan.order))
139	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_deferred", len(plan.deferred))
140	
141	    if not plan.order and not plan.deferred:
142	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "nothing-to-do")
143	        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
144	        return 0
145	
146	    for deferred in plan.deferred:
147	        issue = deferred["issue"]
148	        blocker_id = deferred["blocker_identifier"]
149	        blocker_state = deferred["blocker_state_type"]
150	        print(
151	            f"drain-cycle: deferred {issue['identifier']}"
152	            f" — blocked by {blocker_id} ({blocker_state})",
153	            file=sys.stderr,
154	        )
155	
156	    if not plan.order:
157	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "all-deferred")
158	        return 0
159	
160	    total = len(plan.order)
161	    for index, issue in enumerate(plan.order):
162	        halt_code = _drain_one_issue(
163	            issue,
164	            index=index,
165	            total=total,
166	            repos=repos,
167	            limits=limits,
168	            log=log,
169	            cycle_id=cycle_id,
170	            debug=debug,
171	        )
172	        if halt_code is not None:
173	            return halt_code
174	
175	        # Cycle-wide circuit breaker: every issue may stay under its own
176	        # per-issue caps while their sum drains the quota. Check the
177	        # running totals (which now include the Done issue just finished)
178	        # before spawning the next one; on breach, stop the cycle.
179	        cycle_breach = check_cycle(
180	            limits,
181	            tokens=log.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
182	            cost_usd=log.cycle_cost_usd(),
183	            seconds=log.cycle_duration_seconds(),
184	        )
185	        if cycle_breach is not None:
186	            halt_reason = f"Halt: {cycle_breach.describe()}"
187	            log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
188	            telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-cycle-breach", halt_reason)
189	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
190	            return 1
191	
192	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "drained")
193	    return 0
194	
195	
196	def _drain_one_issue(
197	    issue: dict,
198	    *,
199	    index: int,
200	    total: int,
201	    repos: Repos,
202	    limits: Limits,
203	    log: runlog.RunLog,
204	    cycle_id: str,
205	    debug: bool,
206	) -> int | None:
207	    """Drain a single issue end to end inside a ``drain.issue`` span.
208	
209	    Returns ``None`` when the issue reached Done (the caller then runs the
210	    cycle-wide circuit breaker and proceeds to the next issue), or an exit
211	    code when the run must halt: repo-resolution failure, pre-spawn setup
212	    failure, a per-issue cap breach, or a session that finished not-Done.
213	    Each halt path is also marked on the span with a static ``exception.slug``.
214	    """
215	    identifier = issue["identifier"]
216	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.issue") as issue_span:
217	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.identifier", identifier)
218	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.title", issue["title"])
219	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.index", index + 1)
220	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.total", total)
221	        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
222	
223	        started_at = _now_iso()
224	        try:
225	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
226	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
227	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
228	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
229	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
230	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
231	            halt_reason = (
232	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
233	                f" — {exc}"
234	            )
235	            log.append_entry(
236	                issue_identifier=identifier,
237	                started_at=started_at,
238	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
239	                exit_code=-1,
240	                final_linear_state=state_name,
241	                REDACTED,
242	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
243	            )
244	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
245	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-repo-resolution", halt_reason)
246	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
247	            return 1
248	
249	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.repo", target_repo.name)
250	
251	        try:
252	            worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
253	            # A worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored
254	            # project config (.claude/, .mcp.json) is absent. Symlink it in
255	            # so the worker loads the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP
256	            # as an interactive session at the repo root.
257	            worktree.link_project_config(
258	                target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
259	            )
260	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
261	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
262	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
263	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
264	        except Exception as exc:
265	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
266	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
267	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
268	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
269	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
270	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
271	            halt_reason = (
272	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
273	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
274	            )
275	            log.append_entry(
276	                issue_identifier=identifier,
277	                started_at=started_at,
278	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
279	                exit_code=-1,
280	                final_linear_state=state_name,
281	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
282	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
283	            )
284	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
285	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-setup-failed", halt_reason)
286	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
287	            return 1
288	
289	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)
290	        worker_model = model.resolve(issue)
291	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.model", worker_model)
292	
293	        debug_file = log.debug_path(identifier) if debug else None
294	        if debug_file is not None:
295	            print(
296	                f"drain-cycle: {identifier} debug capture → {debug_file}",
297	                file=sys.stderr,
298	            )
299	
300	        marker: dict = {
301	            "pid": os.getpid(),
302	            "cycle_id": cycle_id,
303	            "run_log_path": str(log.path),
304	            "issue": {
305	                "identifier": identifier,
306	                "title": issue["title"],
307	                "repo": target_repo.name,
308	                "worktree_path": str(worktree_path),
309	            },
310	            "model": worker_model,
311	            "started_at": started_at,
312	            "index": index + 1,
313	            "total": total,
314	            "progress": {},
315	        }
316	        progress.write(marker)
317	
318	        def _make_on_progress(m: dict, ident: str):
319	            def _cb(
320	                turns: int,
321	                cumulative_tokens: int,
322	                peak_context_tokens: int,
323	                cost_usd: float | None,
324	                elapsed_seconds: float,
325	            ) -> None:
326	                m["progress"] = {
327	                    "turns": turns,
328	                    "cumulative_tokens": cumulative_tokens,
329	                    "peak_context_tokens": peak_context_tokens,
330	                    "cost_usd": cost_usd,
331	                    "elapsed_seconds": elapsed_seconds,
332	                    "last_event_at": _now_iso(),
333	                }
334	                progress.write(m)
335	                print(
336	                    progress.format_progress_line(
337	                        ident,
338	                        turns,
339	                        cumulative_tokens,
340	                        peak_context_tokens,
341	                        cost_usd,
342	                        elapsed_seconds,
343	                    ),
344	                    file=sys.stderr,
345	                )
346	            return _cb
347	
348	        try:
349	            result = worker.run_issue(
350	                claude_cmd=_CLAUDE_CMD,
351	                model=worker_model,
352	                prompt=agent_prompt,
353	                cwd=worktree_path,
354	                token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
355	                time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
356	                cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
357	                debug_file=debug_file,
358	                on_progress=_make_on_progress(marker, identifier),
359	            )
360	        finally:
361	            progress.clear()
362	        finished_at = _now_iso()
363	
364	        if result.breach is not None:
365	            # The worker crossed a per-issue cap (tokens or time) and was
366	            # process-group killed (grandchildren reaped). Same revert + halt
367	            # contract as a not-Done exit, with the recorded usage of the
368	            # killed session and the breached cap named in the halt reason.
369	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
370	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
371	                issue["id"],
372	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
373	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
374	            )
375	            halt_reason = (
376	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
377	                f" — {result.breach.describe()}"
378	            )
379	            if revert_error is not None:
380	                halt_reason += (
381	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
382	                )
383	            log.append_entry(
384	                issue_identifier=identifier,
385	                started_at=started_at,
386	                finished_at=finished_at,
387	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
388	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
389	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
390	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
391	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
392	            )
393	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
394	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
395	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-per-issue-breach", halt_reason)
396	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
397	            return 1
398	
399	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
400	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
401	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
402	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
403	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.is_done", is_done)
404	
405	        if is_done:
406	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", post_spawn_state)
407	            remove_error: str | None = None
408	            try:
409	                worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
410	            except RuntimeError as exc:
411	                remove_error = str(exc)
412	                issue_span.set_attribute("worktree.remove_error", remove_error)
413	                print(
414	                    f"drain-cycle: {identifier}: worktree teardown failed: {exc}",
415	                    file=sys.stderr,
416	                )
417	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
418	            log.append_entry(
419	                issue_identifier=identifier,
420	                started_at=started_at,
421	                finished_at=finished_at,
422	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
423	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
424	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
425	                halt_reason=remove_error,
426	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
427	            )
428	            if remove_error is None:
429	                print(f"drain-cycle: {identifier} done; worktree removed.", file=sys.stderr)
430	            return None
431	
432	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
433	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
434	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
435	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
436	            issue["id"],
437	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
438	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
439	        )
440	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
441	        if revert_error is not None:
442	            halt_reason += (
443	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
444	            )
445	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
446	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
447	        log.append_entry(
448	            issue_identifier=identifier,
449	            started_at=started_at,
450	            finished_at=finished_at,
451	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
452	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
453	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
454	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
455	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
456	        )
457	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
458	        telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-issue-not-done", halt_reason)
459	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
460	        return 1
461	
```

1	"""Per-cycle run-log artefact.
2	
3	Each ``drain-cycle`` invocation produces a single JSON file at
4	``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` capturing one
5	entry per attempted issue. The filename embeds the run-start timestamp
6	(UTC, ``%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ``) so re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the
7	same cycle writes a new file instead of clobbering the prior one.
8	Downstream consumers glob the directory and group by ``cycle_id``
9	(carried inside each file).
10	
11	* KR1 (completion %) is computed from ``entries[].final_linear_state``
12	  merged across every file sharing the same ``cycle_id``.
13	* Cycle-drain self-grading reads every file across cycles.
14	* The kill condition (KR1 < 50%) merges per-cycle then thresholds.
15	
16	Schema:
17	
18	::
19	
20	    {
21	      "cycle_id":               "<linear-cycle-uuid>",
22	      "cycle_duration_seconds": <float>,
23	      "cycle_cost_usd":         <float>,
24	      "cycle_tokens_cumulative": <int>,
25	      "cycle_halt_reason":      null | "<cycle-budget halt line>",
26	      "entries":                [
27	        {
28	          "issue_identifier":   "ABA-NNN",
29	          "started_at":         "<iso-8601 UTC>",
30	          "finished_at":        "<iso-8601 UTC>",
31	          "exit_code":          <int>,
32	          "final_linear_state": "Done" | "Todo" | ...,
33	          "worktree_path":      "<absolute path>",
34	          "halt_reason":        null | "<orchestrator stderr halt line>",
35	          "duration_seconds":   <float>,
36	          "model":              null | "<resolved model id>",
37	          "usage":              null | {
38	            "input_tokens":               <int>,
39	            "output_tokens":              <int>,
40	            "cache_creation_input_tokens": <int>,
41	            "cache_read_input_tokens":    <int>,
42	            "cumulative":                 <int>,
43	            "peak_context":               <int>,
44	          },
45	          "cost_usd":           null | <float>,
46	          "num_turns":          null | <int>,
47	          "session_id":         null | "<uuid>",
48	          "is_error":           null | <bool>,
49	        },
50	        ...
51	      ],
52	    }
53	
54	``halt_reason`` is ``null`` on Done entries unless worktree teardown
55	failed after the session completed — in that case the Done entry carries
56	the teardown error string and the drain continues. On the orchestrator's
57	halt entry it is the exact string also written to stderr — both surfaces
58	are produced by the same ``_halt_message`` helper in ``orchestrator.py``
59	so the on-disk and terminal values cannot drift. Non-last entries are
60	``null`` by construction: the orchestrator returns on first halt, so
61	anything before the halt is a Done entry.
62	
63	The worker-usage fields (``model`` through ``is_error``) are populated
64	from the spawned session's stream-json events — see ``worker.py``.
65	Entries written before any session runs (pre-spawn resolution and
66	setup-failure halts) carry ``null`` for ``model`` / ``usage`` /
67	``cost_usd`` / ``num_turns`` / ``session_id`` / ``is_error``; every entry
68	keeps the same key set. ``usage.cumulative`` is the billed-token total
69	across turns; ``usage.peak_context`` is the largest single-turn context.
70	These fields are additive — ``grade.py`` reads only ``cycle_id`` and
71	``entries[].final_linear_state`` / ``exit_code``, so existing run logs
72	without them grade unchanged.
73	
74	``cycle_cost_usd`` and ``cycle_tokens_cumulative`` are per-invocation
75	totals over ``entries`` (entries with ``null`` cost or usage contribute
76	zero), recomputed on every persist alongside ``cycle_duration_seconds``.
77	
78	``cycle_halt_reason`` is ``null`` unless the orchestrator stopped the run
79	because a *cycle-wide* cap (tokens / cost / wall-clock) was breached — the
80	death-by-aggregate case where every issue stayed under its own per-issue
81	caps but their sum crossed the cycle budget. The breaching issue's own
82	entry is recorded normally (it ran to Done); this top-level field is the
83	record of why no further issue was attempted, carrying the same ``Halt:``
84	string printed to stderr. A per-issue breach, by contrast, lands in the
85	halting entry's ``halt_reason`` like any other halt.
86	
87	``cycle_duration_seconds`` is computed automatically on every persist as
88	``max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`` across ``entries`` (``0.0`` when
89	empty). This is the KR2 grading proxy — "how long was the agent doing
90	execution for me unattended" stands in for "operator hands-off time" —
91	and replaces the originally specified ``time_spent`` block, which
92	required manual self-report at cycle close and was never going to be
93	filled in.
94	
95	Persistence is incremental: ``append_entry`` re-serialises the whole dict
96	on every call, so a mid-run crash still leaves a well-formed file with
97	every issue attempted so far. Cycle scale (≤ 15 issues) makes the cost
98	irrelevant.
99	"""
100	from __future__ import annotations
101	
102	import json
103	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
104	from datetime import datetime, timezone
105	from pathlib import Path
106	from typing import Any
107	
108	
109	def runs_dir() -> Path:
110	    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written.
111	
112	    Resolved on every call (not module-import time) so tests can redirect
113	    via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`` after the module is imported.
114	    """
115	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
116	
117	
118	@dataclass
119	class RunLog:
120	    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start.
121	
122	    Writing the initial ``{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds: 0.0,
123	    entries: []}`` shell in ``__post_init__`` means that even a zero-issue
124	    cycle (or a crash before the first ``append_entry``) leaves the
125	    artefact on disk, which is what kill-condition tooling needs to
126	    distinguish "drained nothing" from "never ran".
127	
128	    The filename embeds a UTC run-start timestamp with microsecond
129	    resolution, so two ``RunLog`` instances on the same ``cycle_id``
130	    (re-running ``drain-cycle`` after a halt) write to two separate
131	    files instead of one clobbering the other.
132	    """
133	
134	    cycle_id: str
135	    path: Path = field(init=False)
136	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
137	    cycle_halt_reason: str | None = field(default=None, init=False)
138	
139	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
140	        directory = runs_dir()
141	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
142	        run_started_at = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)
143	        timestamp = run_started_at.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ")
144	        self.path = directory / f"{self.cycle_id}-{timestamp}.json"
145	        self._persist()
146	
147	    def append_entry(
148	        self,
149	        *,
150	        issue_identifier: str,
151	        started_at: str,
152	        finished_at: str,
153	        exit_code: int,
154	        final_linear_state: str,
155	        worktree_path: str,
156	        halt_reason: str | None = None,
157	        duration_seconds: float | None = None,
158	        model: str | None = None,
159	        usage: dict[str, int] | None = None,
160	        cost_usd: float | None = None,
161	        num_turns: int | None = None,
162	        session_id: str | None = None,
163	        is_error: bool | None = None,
164	    ) -> None:
165	        if duration_seconds is None:
166	            duration_seconds = (
167	                datetime.fromisoformat(finished_at)
168	                - datetime.fromisoformat(started_at)
169	            ).total_seconds()
170	        self.entries.append(
171	            {
172	                "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
173	                "started_at": started_at,
174	                "finished_at": finished_at,
175	                "exit_code": exit_code,
176	                "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
177	                "worktree_path": worktree_path,
178	                "halt_reason": halt_reason,
179	                "duration_seconds": duration_seconds,
180	                "model": model,
181	                "usage": usage,
182	                "cost_usd": cost_usd,
183	                "num_turns": num_turns,
184	                "session_id": session_id,
185	                "is_error": is_error,
186	            }
187	        )
188	        self._persist()
189	
190	    def debug_path(self, issue_identifier: str) -> Path:
191	        """Path for one issue's opt-in ``--debug-file`` capture, beside the log.
192	
193	        Named ``<run-log-stem>-<issue-identifier>.debug.log`` so each issue's
194	        startup diagnostics sit next to the run log they belong to and the
195	        run-start timestamp keeps re-runs from clobbering a prior capture.
196	        Only written when debug capture is enabled — see ``orchestrator.run``
197	        and ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10.
198	        """
199	        return self.path.with_name(f"{self.path.stem}-{issue_identifier}.debug.log")
200	
201	    def set_cycle_halt(self, reason: str) -> None:
202	        """Record why a cycle-wide cap stopped the run, and persist.
203	
204	        Called by the orchestrator after the breaching issue's own entry is
205	        already written, so the file carries both the issue's normal entry
206	        and the cycle-level reason no further issue was attempted.
207	        """
208	        self.cycle_halt_reason = reason
209	        self._persist()
210	
211	    def cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
212	        if not self.entries:
213	            return 0.0
214	        starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
215	        finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
216	        return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
217	
218	    def cycle_cost_usd(self) -> float:
219	        return sum(
220	            e["cost_usd"] for e in self.entries if e.get("cost_usd") is not None
221	        )
222	
223	    def cycle_tokens_cumulative(self) -> int:
224	        return sum(
225	            e["usage"]["cumulative"]
226	            for e in self.entries
227	            if e.get("usage") is not None
228	        )
229	
230	    def _persist(self) -> None:
231	        payload = {
232	            "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
233	            "cycle_duration_seconds": self.cycle_duration_seconds(),
234	            "cycle_cost_usd": self.cycle_cost_usd(),
235	            "cycle_tokens_cumulative": self.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
236	            "cycle_halt_reason": self.cycle_halt_reason,
237	            "entries": self.entries,
238	        }
239	        self.path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
240	
```

1	"""Tests for the ``worktree`` git wrapper.
2	
3	The happy-path is exercised end-to-end by ``test_orchestrator_iteration``.
4	What these tests pin is the failure mode: git's stderr must be captured
5	and surfaced in the raised ``RuntimeError`` so the operator (and the
6	runlog ``halt_reason`` written by the orchestrator) sees what actually
7	went wrong, not just a non-zero exit code.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import os
12	import subprocess
13	from pathlib import Path
14	
15	import pytest
16	
17	from drain_cycle import worktree
18	
19	
20	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
21	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
22	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.email", "t@x"], cwd=repo, check=True)
23	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "T"], cwd=repo, check=True)
24	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
25	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
26	    subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
27	
28	
29	def _commit_gitignore(repo: Path, *patterns: str) -> None:
30	    (repo / ".gitignore").write_text("\n".join(patterns) + "\n")
31	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", ".gitignore"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
32	    subprocess.run(
33	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "gitignore"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
34	    )
35	
36	
37	def test_add_failure_raises_with_git_stderr_in_message(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
38	    """``git worktree add`` fails when the branch name is already taken.
39	    The raised RuntimeError must carry git's stderr so the operator can
40	    diagnose without re-running by hand."""
41	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
42	    repo.mkdir()
43	    _init_repo(repo)
44	
45	    # First add succeeds, taking the branch "ABA-X".
46	    worktree.add(repo, "ABA-X")
47	    # Second add fails — same branch.
48	    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
49	        worktree.add(repo, "ABA-X")
50	
51	    msg = str(excinfo.value)
52	    # The message names the operation and includes git's actual complaint
53	    # (typically "already exists" or "already checked out").
54	    assert "worktree add" in msg
55	    assert "ABA-X" in msg
56	
57	
58	def test_remove_failure_raises_with_git_stderr_in_message(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
59	    """``git worktree remove`` on a non-existent path fails. The raised
60	    RuntimeError must carry git's stderr."""
61	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
62	    repo.mkdir()
63	    _init_repo(repo)
64	
65	    bogus = repo / ".worktrees" / "never-existed"
66	    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
67	        worktree.remove(repo, bogus)
68	
69	    msg = str(excinfo.value)
70	    assert "worktree remove" in msg
71	    # Either git's own "is not a working tree" message or the path itself.
72	    assert str(bogus) in msg or "working tree" in msg.lower()
73	
74	
75	def test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
76	    """A gitignored ``.claude/`` (absent from the worktree checkout) is
77	    symlinked back to the repo's real dir, so the worker reads through it."""
78	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
79	    repo.mkdir()
80	    _init_repo(repo)
81	    _commit_gitignore(repo, ".claude", ".worktrees")
82	    (repo / ".claude").mkdir()
83	    (repo / ".claude" / "settings.json").write_text('{"hooks": {}}\n')
84	
85	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
86	    created = worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt, [".claude"])
87	
88	    assert created == [wt / ".claude"]
89	    assert (wt / ".claude").is_symlink()
90	    assert Path(os.readlink(wt / ".claude")) == repo.resolve() / ".claude"
91	    assert (wt / ".claude" / "settings.json").read_text() == '{"hooks": {}}\n'
92	
93	
94	def test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
95	    """A repo without the named config produces no link and no error."""
96	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
97	    repo.mkdir()
98	    _init_repo(repo)
99	    _commit_gitignore(repo, ".worktrees")
100	
101	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
102	    created = worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt, [".claude", ".mcp.json"])
103	
104	    assert created == []
105	    assert not os.path.lexists(wt / ".claude")
106	    assert not os.path.lexists(wt / ".mcp.json")
107	
108	
109	def test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
110	    """A tracked ``.claude/`` git already checked out is left as a real dir,
111	    never clobbered by a symlink."""
112	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
113	    repo.mkdir()
114	    _init_repo(repo)
115	    (repo / ".claude").mkdir()
116	    (repo / ".claude" / "settings.json").write_text("tracked\n")
117	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", ".claude"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
118	    subprocess.run(
119	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "track claude"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
120	    )
121	
122	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
123	    created = worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt, [".claude"])
124	
125	    assert created == []
126	    assert not (wt / ".claude").is_symlink()
127	    assert (wt / ".claude" / "settings.json").read_text() == "tracked\n"
128	
129	
130	def test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present(
131	    tmp_path: Path,
132	) -> None:
133	    """Every present, gitignored name is linked; absent ones are skipped."""
134	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
135	    repo.mkdir()
136	    _init_repo(repo)
137	    _commit_gitignore(repo, ".claude", ".mcp.json", ".entire", ".worktrees")
138	    (repo / ".mcp.json").write_text("{}\n")
139	    (repo / ".entire").mkdir()
140	    (repo / ".entire" / "state").write_text("x\n")
141	
142	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
143	    created = worktree.link_project_config(
144	        repo, wt, [".claude", ".mcp.json", ".entire"]
145	    )
146	
147	    assert set(created) == {wt / ".mcp.json", wt / ".entire"}
148	    assert (wt / ".mcp.json").is_symlink()
149	    assert (wt / ".entire").is_symlink()
150	    assert not os.path.lexists(wt / ".claude")
151	
152	
153	def test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
154	    """The linked entry is gitignored in the worktree (it shares the repo's
155	    tracked .gitignore), so a worker's ``git add`` never stages it."""
156	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
157	    repo.mkdir()
158	    _init_repo(repo)
159	    _commit_gitignore(repo, ".claude", ".worktrees")
160	    (repo / ".claude").mkdir()
161	    (repo / ".claude" / "settings.json").write_text("{}\n")
162	
163	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
164	    worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt, [".claude"])
165	
166	    status = subprocess.run(
167	        ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
168	        cwd=wt,
169	        check=True,
170	        capture_output=True,
171	        text=True,
172	    )
173	    assert ".claude" not in status.stdout
174	
175	
176	def test_remove_preserves_symlink_target(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
177	    """Removing the worktree deletes the symlink, not the repo's real dir."""
178	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
179	    repo.mkdir()
180	    _init_repo(repo)
181	    _commit_gitignore(repo, ".claude", ".worktrees")
182	    (repo / ".claude").mkdir()
183	    (repo / ".claude" / "settings.json").write_text("keep me\n")
184	
185	    wt = worktree.add(repo, "ABA-A")
186	    worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt, [".claude"])
187	    worktree.remove(repo, wt)
188	
189	    assert (repo / ".claude").is_dir()
190	    assert (repo / ".claude" / "settings.json").read_text() == "keep me\n"

1	"""Prompt-template assertions.
2	
3	The prompt is what the orchestrator hands to ``claude -p`` — its four
4	segments (title, body, execution preamble, tail line) must appear in the
5	documented order or the spawned agent loses the context it needs to
6	complete the issue and self-transition Linear. These tests pin the
7	ordering and the load-bearing substrings so a future refactor cannot
8	silently reshape the contract.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	
14	from drain_cycle.prompt import _TAIL, build
15	
16	
17	def _fixture_issue() -> dict:
18	    return {
19	        "id": "id-ABA-999",
20	        "identifier": "ABA-999",
21	        "title": "Fixture title — drain a trivial issue",
22	        "description": "Fixture body.\n\nMultiple paragraphs preserved verbatim.",
23	        "priority": 3,
24	        "sortOrder": 1.0,
25	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
26	    }
27	
28	
29	def _positions(text: str, *needles: str) -> list[int]:
30	    """Return the index of each needle, asserting each one is present."""
31	    found = []
32	    for needle in needles:
33	        idx = text.find(needle)
34	        assert idx != -1, f"missing segment: {needle!r}\n--- prompt ---\n{text}"
35	        found.append(idx)
36	    return found
37	
38	
39	def test_prompt_contains_four_segments_in_order(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
40	    issue = _fixture_issue()
41	    worktree = tmp_path / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"]
42	    rendered = build(issue, worktree)
43	
44	    title_idx, body_idx, preamble_idx, tail_idx = _positions(
45	        rendered,
46	        f"# {issue['title']}",
47	        issue["description"],
48	        "Execution instructions:",
49	        _TAIL,
50	    )
51	    assert title_idx < body_idx < preamble_idx < tail_idx
52	
53	
54	def test_preamble_names_worktree_base_branch_and_mcp_call(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
55	    issue = _fixture_issue()
56	    worktree = tmp_path / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"]
57	    rendered = build(issue, worktree)
58	
59	    # Each of the three preamble facts the spawned agent needs to act on.
60	    assert str(worktree) in rendered
61	    assert "Base branch: main" in rendered
62	    assert "mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue" in rendered
63	    assert 'state: "Done"' in rendered
64	    # The Linear MCP call needs to know which issue — include the identifier.
65	    assert issue["identifier"] in rendered
66	
67	
68	def test_tail_line_is_the_last_non_empty_line(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
69	    issue = _fixture_issue()
70	    worktree = tmp_path / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"]
71	    rendered = build(issue, worktree)
72	
73	    non_empty = [line for line in rendered.splitlines() if line.strip()]
74	    assert non_empty[-1] == _TAIL
75	
76	
77	def test_empty_description_does_not_break_rendering(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
78	    issue = _fixture_issue()
79	    issue["description"] = None  # Linear can return null descriptions
80	    worktree = tmp_path / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"]
81	    rendered = build(issue, worktree)
82	
83	    # Title and preamble must still render in order even with no body.
84	    title_idx, preamble_idx, tail_idx = _positions(
85	        rendered,
86	        f"# {issue['title']}",
87	        "Execution instructions:",
88	        _TAIL,
89	    )
90	    assert title_idx < preamble_idx < tail_idx
91

1	"""Unit tests for the guardrail thresholds and their loader.
2	
3	These exercise pure logic — no subprocess, no Linear. The breach *kills*
4	are exercised against a real process group in ``test_worker.py`` (per-issue)
5	and through the orchestrator in ``test_orchestrator_cycle_limit.py``
6	(cycle-wide). Here we pin: the defaults, the ``limits.yml`` precedence
7	(absent → default, ``null`` → off, number → override), the malformed-config
8	rejections, the ``check_cycle`` ordering, and the ``Breach`` wording.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	
14	import pytest
15	
16	from drain_cycle import limits
17	
18	
19	def _write(tmp_path: Path, body: str) -> Path:
20	    path = tmp_path / "limits.yml"
21	    path.write_text(body)
22	    return path
23	
24	
25	def test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_absent(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
26	    loaded = limits.load(tmp_path / "nonexistent.yml")
27	    assert loaded == limits.Limits()
28	    # The documented defaults, pinned so a careless edit to the dataclass
29	    # surfaces here.
30	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens == 8_000_000
31	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == 20 * 60
32	    assert loaded.per_issue_cost_usd == 15.0
33	    assert loaded.cycle_tokens == 30_000_000
34	    assert loaded.cycle_seconds == 90 * 60
35	    assert loaded.cycle_cost_usd == 60.0
36	
37	
38	def test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_empty(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
39	    assert limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "")) == limits.Limits()
40	
41	
42	def test_load_overrides_only_named_keys_keeping_other_defaults(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
43	    loaded = limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: 1000000\ncycle_cost_usd: 25\n"))
44	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens == 1_000_000
45	    assert loaded.cycle_cost_usd == 25
46	    # Untouched keys keep their baked-in defaults.
47	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == limits.Limits().per_issue_seconds
48	    assert loaded.cycle_tokens == limits.Limits().cycle_tokens
49	
50	
51	def test_load_null_value_turns_a_guardrail_off(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
52	    loaded = limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: null\nper_issue_cost_usd: null\n"))
53	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens is None
54	    assert loaded.per_issue_cost_usd is None
55	    # A sibling guardrail stays at its default — "off" is per-key.
56	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == limits.Limits().per_issue_seconds
57	
58	
59	def test_load_rejects_non_mapping(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
60	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="mapping of limit names"):
61	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "- just\n- a\n- list\n"))
62	
63	
64	def test_load_rejects_unknown_key(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
65	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="unknown keys: per_issue_dollars"):
66	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_dollars: 5\n"))
67	
68	
69	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
70	    "value",
71	    ["0", "-1", "abc", "true"],
72	)
73	def test_load_rejects_non_positive_or_non_numeric(tmp_path: Path, value: str) -> None:
74	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="positive number or null"):
75	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, f"per_issue_tokens: {value}\n"))
76	
77	
78	def test_load_rejects_invalid_yaml(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
79	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="not valid YAML"):
80	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: [unclosed\n"))
81	
82	
83	def test_check_cycle_no_breach_under_all_caps() -> None:
84	    lim = limits.Limits()
85	    assert limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=1, cost_usd=0.5, seconds=10.0) is None
86	
87	
88	def test_check_cycle_tokens_take_precedence_as_primary_guardrail() -> None:
89	    # Both tokens and cost are over their caps; tokens is reported first.
90	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=100, cycle_cost_usd=10.0)
91	    breach = limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=200, cost_usd=50.0, seconds=0.0)
92	    assert breach is not None
93	    assert breach.scope == "cycle"
94	    assert breach.metric == "token"
95	    assert breach.limit == 100
96	    assert breach.observed == 200
97	
98	
99	def test_check_cycle_reports_cost_then_time() -> None:
100	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=10.0, cycle_seconds=5.0)
101	    cost_breach = limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=999, cost_usd=12.0, seconds=99.0)
102	    assert cost_breach is not None and cost_breach.metric == "cost"
103	
104	    lim_time_only = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=None, cycle_seconds=5.0)
105	    time_breach = limits.check_cycle(lim_time_only, tokens=999, cost_usd=999.0, seconds=6.0)
106	    assert time_breach is not None and time_breach.metric == "time"
107	
108	
109	def test_check_cycle_skips_disabled_guardrails() -> None:
110	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=None, cycle_seconds=None)
111	    assert limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=10**12, cost_usd=10**6, seconds=10**6) is None
112	
113	
114	def test_breach_describe_wording() -> None:
115	    assert (
116	        limits.Breach("per-issue", "token", 8_000_000, 9_000_000).describe()
117	        == "per-issue token cap exceeded: 9,000,000 ≥ 8,000,000 tokens"
118	    )
119	    assert (
120	        limits.Breach("cycle", "cost", 60.0, 62.5).describe()
121	        == "cycle cost cap exceeded: $62.50 ≥ $60.00"
122	    )
123	    assert (
124	        limits.Breach("per-issue", "time", 1200, 1201).describe()
125	        == "per-issue time cap exceeded: 1,201s ≥ 1,200s"
126	    )
127
