{"id":"ABA-299","title":"Resume a halted issue on re-run (reuse the preserved worktree)","description":"## What\n\nOn re-run after a halt, drain-cycle will **reuse the preserved worktree** instead of failing on the existing branch, and spawn a **fresh** `claude` session whose prompt says \"prior work is committed on this branch — review it and continue from where it left off.\" A fresh session means a **clean context, so the per-issue token budget resets** (the whole reason the operator raised the cap). On Done, the existing teardown removes the worktree as normal.\n\n**Scope.** The change is deliberately narrow: automatic only for the clean post-halt state (registered worktree + branch); bounded by a resume-attempt cap; preserves a dirty tree as-is (never resets/cleans); not in-process auto-retry. The manual escape hatch (delete worktree + branch by hand) remains the way to force a fresh start.\n\n## Why\n\n### The mechanics today\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns one `claude -p` worker per Linear issue in an isolated git worktree at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<id>` on a branch named `<id>`. When a worker is SIGKILLed mid-issue (the dominant case: a per-issue **token-cap breach**), the orchestrator reverts the issue's Linear state to its pre-run value (e.g. `Todo`, so it is re-picked next invocation) but **leaves the worktree and branch in place** for inspection (`docs/design-decisions.md` §3).\n\n### The gap\n\nOn re-run, the issue is re-picked, but `worktree.add` runs `git worktree add -b <id> …`, which fails because that branch already exists. Today this surfaces as a clean `Halt: … — setup failed: … a branch named '<id>' already exists` (the generic pre-spawn handler already turns the `RuntimeError` into spec-shaped UX — so the \"detect cleanly\" goal of the superseded <issue id=\"3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b\">ABA-231</issue> is effectively met). But the run cannot proceed: the operator must manually delete the worktree/branch, **discarding any committed work the killed session produced**.\n\n### Motivating case\n\nA token-breached run on <issue id=\"f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c\">ABA-276</issue> had already committed 2 commits / 142 lines to the `ABA-276` branch before being killed. That work was the durable record of progress — and the only way forward was to throw it away and re-drain from scratch (re-spending \\~8M tokens).\n\n### The design decision this reverses\n\n`docs/design-decisions.md` §8 currently pins: *\"Retry. A halted issue is not retried automatically. The operator inspects the worktree and decides — fix, redo manually, or descope.\"* This work **partially reverses** that and so must add a new **§14** that supersedes the §8 \"Retry\" bullet (per the \"memory lives in artefacts / ADRs are superseded, not silently ignored\" rule). (§13 is already taken by \"Opt-in OpenTelemetry tracing to Honeycomb\", which landed after this issue was shaped — the next free section number is §14; the agent should confirm the highest section at implementation time.) The reversal is narrow and must be documented as such:\n\n* Resume reuses a **preserved, committed** work-in-progress on a **manual re-run** (or scripted re-run). It is **not** in-process auto-retry, and it is **bounded** by a resume-attempt cap (below).\n* §3 (worktree preservation on halt) and §10 (`link_project_config`) are **relied upon**, not changed.\n* The `docs/tasks/rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md` framing (\"no auto-cleanup, operator owns retry; detect-and-halt only\") is superseded by this work; note it in §14. (The corresponding Linear issue, <issue id=\"3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b\">ABA-231</issue>, is already Canceled/archived — this issue replaces its stance.)\n\n### Why not `claude --resume`\n\nA SIGKILLed worker has `session_id: null` in the run log (it dies before the terminal `result` event), so `--resume` isn't even available. And `--resume` would reload the exact context that blew the token budget, re-breaching almost immediately. The committed branch — not the session — is the correct resume point.\n\n### Assumptions (the core bet)\n\n* **A1.** The dominant halt is a token-cap breach, and a killed worker has usually committed incremental work to the `<id>` branch (true in the <issue id=\"f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c\">ABA-276</issue> case: 2 commits). If a worker dies before committing anything, resume re-runs from an empty branch — no worse than today, and bounded by the cap (A3).\n* **A2.** Only the clean post-halt state (registered worktree + branch) is worth auto-handling. Operator-induced partial states (branch without worktree, orphan dir) are rare and acceptably handled by the **existing** clean halt — no new code. This is the main simplification; if partial-state auto-recovery turns out to be wanted, it's an additive follow-up.\n* **A3.** A fresh session, pointed at committed work and told to continue, will build on it rather than restart. This is the core bet; the resume prompt directive (`git log`/`git status` first) is the cheap insurance, and the resume-attempt cap is the backstop if the bet fails for a given issue.\n\n## How\n\n### Resolved design choices\n\n* **Resume is automatic on re-run** (the operator's explicit intent). Forcing a fresh start is the manual escape hatch: delete the worktree + branch (`git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`), exactly as we just did for <issue id=\"f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c\">ABA-276</issue>. No new flag needed.\n* **Resume-attempt cap = 3** (configurable). After 3 non-Done attempts of an issue within the cycle, drain-cycle halts **before spawning** (zero token burn) and leaves the issue for manual intervention. This is the only backstop: the cycle-wide token cap does **not** fire on repeated halts — the breach path `return 1`s before `check_cycle`, and each re-run is a new process with a fresh `RunLog`, so cumulative cycle tokens reset every invocation.\n* **Dirty preserved tree: preserve as-is.** The fresh session reconciles uncommitted edits via `git status`/`git log`. Never `reset --hard`/`clean` (would destroy the saved work and the gitignored config symlinks). Never switch teardown to `--force`.\n\n### Worktree reuse logic (two branches, not five)\n\nFor picked issue `<id>` in `target_repo`, with `path = target_repo/.worktrees/<id>`:\n\n1. **A worktree is already registered at** `path` → reuse it. Run **no git command** — just return `(path, resumed=True)`. This is the clean post-halt state (the <issue id=\"f8927151-bb4d-4af7-9410-071b39c8577c\">ABA-276</issue> case) and the only state worth auto-handling.\n2. **Otherwise** → call today's `add` (`git worktree add -b <id> <path> main`), returning `(path, resumed=False)`.\n\nThat's it. The rare leftover states — branch exists but no worktree, or an orphan directory at `path` — make `add` raise `RuntimeError` (with git's stderr), which the orchestrator's **existing** pre-spawn handler already turns into the clean `Halt: … — setup failed: …` line. We do **not** add bespoke detection, `prune`, branch re-checkout, or a new exception type for those: they're operator-induced, rare, and the operator clears the leftover by hand (`git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`). **The detection is one git call** (`git worktree list --porcelain`, parsed for a record at `path.resolve()`) plus the existing `add`.\n\nVerified against real git: any `git worktree add` onto an already-registered path fails, so the reuse case must **not** call `add` — hence the registration check first. Reuse is a pure no-op on git state, so a dirty index / staged / untracked files in the preserved worktree survive untouched (this is the \"preserve as-is\" choice, for free).\n\n### Component changes\n\n#### 1\\. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new `ensure` (keep `add` as-is)\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass WorktreeHandle:\n    path: Path\n    resumed: bool\n\ndef ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> WorktreeHandle:\n    \"\"\"Reuse a preserved worktree if one is registered at the expected\n    path, else create a fresh one via add(). Reuse runs no git command.\"\"\"\n    path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier\n    if _is_registered_worktree(repo, path):\n        return WorktreeHandle(path, resumed=True)\n    return WorktreeHandle(add(repo, identifier), resumed=False)\n```\n\n* One private helper: `_is_registered_worktree(repo, path) -> bool` — runs `git worktree list --porcelain` (via the existing `_run_git`-style call) and checks for a `worktree` record equal to `path.resolve()`. Annotate the §8 Retry bullet as superseded by §14. (Confirm the next free section number at implementation time — §13 is OpenTelemetry tracing.)\n* `README.md`: the halt example currently says the worktree is \"left in place\"; add that a re-run now **resumes** it, and document `max_resume_attempts` alongside the other `limits.yml` keys.\n* `docs/tasks/rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md`: note it is superseded by this feature.\n\n### Test plan\n\n(Mirrors existing patterns: real git in tmp repos; monkeypatched Linear + fake-claude shell script; no conftest.) `pytest` **green is the per-PR-layer precondition** (see Delivery) — the unit and integration tests below are the layer-by-layer gate, distinct from the behavioural Acceptance criteria.\n\n`tests/test_worktree.py` (real git, existing `_init_repo`):\n\n* fresh → `WorktreeHandle(resumed=False)`, worktree+branch created.\n* reuse registered worktree → call `ensure` once, commit + stage + leave untracked files, call `ensure` again → same path, `resumed=True`, and `git status --porcelain` / `git log` unchanged (pins the no-op-on-git guarantee).\n* leftover branch without worktree (operator removed the worktree) → `ensure` falls through to `add`, which raises `RuntimeError` (confirms leftover states hit the existing halt path, not a crash).\n* `link_project_config` idempotent on reuse → returns `[]`, symlink intact.\n\n`tests/test_orchestrator_resume.py` (new; mirror `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`):\n\n* **headline**: pre-seed worktree+branch+commit for the first issue, stub it `Todo`, fake-claude marks it Done → exit 0, **no worktree-add failure**, worktree removed on Done, entry `final_linear_state == \"Done\"`.\n* resumed prompt mentions prior work: prompt-capturing fake-claude on a pre-seeded worktree → spawned prompt contains the resume directive and `<id>`.\n* resume cap: pre-seed 3 prior non-Done entries for `<id>` in `runs/<cycle-id>-*.json`, `max_resume_attempts=3` → exit 1 with the cap halt reason, `set_state` not called, no spawn.\n\n`tests/test_prompt.py`: `resumed=True` includes the resume directive + identifier and `_TAIL` is still the last non-empty line; `resumed=False` is byte-identical to today's prompt.\n\n`tests/test_limits.py`: `max_resume_attempts` honours absent-key default (3), explicit `null` (off), positive override, and rejects bool/non-positive via `_coerce`.\n\n### Task breakdown\n\n1. `worktree.py`: `WorktreeHandle`, `_is_registered_worktree`, `ensure`. `add` untouched.\n2. `tests/test_worktree.py`: reuse + leftover-falls-through tests — get git mechanics green first.\n3. `prompt.py` + `tests/test_prompt.py`: resume variant.\n4. `limits.py` + `tests/test_limits.py`: `max_resume_attempts`.\n5. `orchestrator.py`: swap `add`→`ensure`, thread `resumed`, add `_resume_attempts` + cap guard.\n6. `tests/test_orchestrator_resume.py`: integration tests.\n7. `docs/design-decisions.md` §14, `README.md`, `rerun-after-halt-detect-cleanly.md` note.\n\n## Delivery — author as a 4-PR Graphite stack\n\n**This issue is not drained via drain-cycle and is not split into Linear sub-issues.** It is authored by **one supervised agent in a single session**, working on a branch off `main` in the repo root (not a `.worktrees/` sandbox), and delivered as a **Graphite (**`gt`**) PR stack** — one PR per layer, reviewed bottom→top. This is the owner-authorised exception to the AGENTS.md \"push directly to main; PRs only when the owner asks\" default. <issue id=\"21915a3a-c443-4c9e-a032-b0964f0ed845\">ABA-299</issue> stays the single tracked issue.\n\n**Precondition:** `gt` (1.8.x) authenticated against `origin` (`github.com/ababushkin/drain-cycle`); the session is supervised, so branch/PR creation runs with approvals. The repo has **no CI**, so each layer's gate is a local `pytest` run — not a remote check.\n\n**The stack (maps the 7 tasks above onto 4 PRs):**\n\n| PR | Layer | Tasks | Contents | Green on its own |\n| -- | -- | -- | -- | -- |\n| PR1 | worktree primitive | 1, 2 | `WorktreeHandle`, `_is_registered_worktree`, `ensure`; `test_worktree.py` reuse/leftover tests. `add` untouched. | yes (its unit tests) |\n| PR2 | prompt variant | 3 | `prompt.build(resumed=)` + resume segment; `test_prompt.py`. | yes |\n| PR3 | limits knob | 4 | `max_resume_attempts` on `Limits`; `test_limits.py`. | yes |\n| PR4 | wiring + docs | 5, 6, 7 | orchestrator `add`→`ensure`, thread `resumed`, `_resume_attempts` + cap guard; `test_orchestrator_resume.py`; **and docs (§14, README, supersession note) in this same PR**. | yes (full suite) |\n\nPR1–3 are independent leaves (different files), each green on its own even though no production caller uses them yet. PR4 is the only behaviour-changing layer and integrates the three below it. **Docs land in PR4** so the §8-reversal ADR ships in the same PR as the behaviour that reverses it (an architectural change updates its ADR in the same PR — don't trail the docs in a later PR).\n\n**Authoring loop — one layer at a time, gated:**\n\n1. `gt create` a branch for the layer (conventional-commit subject, e.g. `feat: worktree.ensure reuses a preserved worktree`).\n2. Run `pytest` — **the layer's own tests must be green** before submitting (no CI; local pytest is the only gate).\n3. `gt submit` — opens the real GitHub PR for that layer on `origin`.\n4. **STOP and report the PR; wait for the operator's go-ahead.** Do **not** start the next layer until approved.\n5. On approval, build the next layer **on top** with `gt create` (never branch from `main` for layers 2–4 — the stack is one lineage; `gt` sets the parent).\n6. On review feedback for a lower layer: `gt down`, amend, `gt restack`, `gt submit` to update the stack. The same agent restacks the whole tower — it has full context, which is the reason one agent authors all four layers rather than handing each to a fresh worker.\n\n**Completion ritual is adapted for the stack:** `/code-review-and-quality` + commit happen **per layer** (before each `gt submit`); the Linear issue moves to **Done once the whole stack is approved and merged**, not per layer. Do not push individual layers straight to `main`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\nObservable end-to-end checks, verified after the stack merges. (`pytest` green is the per-PR-layer precondition — see Test plan — not a behavioural criterion.)\n\n* **Headline resume.** In a throwaway repo, `worktree.add` an issue branch, commit a file, leave the worktree; run the orchestrator (with a fake/cheap model) against a stubbed cycle containing that issue → it resumes (prompt mentions prior work), completes, and the worktree is removed on Done.\n* **Token budget resets.** The resumed run's log entry has a non-null, distinct `session_id` and `cumulative_tokens` reflecting only this session's spend — not the killed run's.\n* **Manual escape hatch.** After `git worktree remove … && git branch -D <id>`, the next drain creates a fresh worktree (`resumed=False`) and runs normally.\n* **Dirty preserved tree.** With staged + untracked edits left in the preserved worktree, drain runs and the resumed session sees them via `git status` — no `reset`/`clean` ran; the symlinked config dirs survive.\n* **Leftover partial state.** With the worktree directory deleted but the branch kept, drain halts cleanly with `Halt: … — setup failed: …` (no traceback, no Linear transition).\n* **Resume cap fires.** Seed 3 non-Done run-log entries for an id; the next run halts before spawn with the cap reason, `set_state` not called.\n* **Cap disable.** With `max_resume_attempts: null` in `limits.yml` and ≥3 seeded non-Done entries, drain proceeds rather than halting.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n(Deliberate, v2 candidates.)\n\n* **Auto-recovery of operator-induced partial states** (branch without worktree → re-checkout; orphan/prunable dir → prune). Deliberately cut for simplicity (A2): these hit the existing clean halt and the operator clears the leftover by hand. Add only if it proves needed.\n* Per-resume token budget (a tighter cap for resumed sessions) — the count cap is the load-bearing guard for v1.\n* Skipping a capped issue to drain the rest of the cycle (today any halt stops the run; a capped issue blocks the cycle until the operator intervenes — consistent with the existing halt contract).\n* A `drain-cycle clean <id>` recovery subcommand.\n* `claude --resume` session continuation (unavailable on kill; undesirable for token-breach).\n\n## Supersedes\n\nReplaces the \"operator owns retry / detect-and-halt only\" stance of <issue id=\"3c8d126f-d01d-4a8e-a7ac-faa69dc6027b\">ABA-231</issue> (already Canceled). This issue is the resume-enabled successor: it reuses the preserved worktree rather than just halting cleanly on re-run.","priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-299/resume-a-halted-issue-on-re-run-reuse-the-preserved-worktree","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-299-resume-a-halted-issue-on-re-run-reuse-the-preserved-worktree","createdAt":"2026-05-26T11:51:39.245Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-28T02:30:58.568Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":"2026-05-28T02:30:58.502Z","canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"In Progress","statusType":"started","labels":["drain-cycle","opus","high"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","assignee":"Anton Babushkin","assigneeId":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"52563a03-080e-4db7-b003-ebd27759cf29"}

1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main`` per
4	README §3, used once, then removed on Done — or preserved on halt so a
5	later re-run can resume against the committed work (see
6	``docs/design-decisions.md`` §14).
7	
8	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
9	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
10	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
11	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
12	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import os
17	import subprocess
18	from dataclasses import dataclass
19	from pathlib import Path
20	from typing import Iterable
21	
22	from . import telemetry
23	
24	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
25	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
26	
27	
28	@dataclass(frozen=True)
29	class WorktreeHandle:
30	    """A prepared worktree, together with whether it was reused.
31	
32	    ``resumed`` is ``True`` when ``ensure`` found a pre-existing worktree
33	    registered at the expected path (typically left behind by an earlier
34	    halted run). Callers thread the flag into the spawn-time prompt so
35	    the agent knows it is continuing from prior committed work rather
36	    than starting fresh.
37	    """
38	
39	    path: Path
40	    resumed: bool
41	
42	
43	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
44	    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.
45	
46	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
47	    """
48	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
49	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.add") as span:
50	        span.set_attribute("worktree.identifier", identifier)
51	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
52	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
53	        _run_git(
54	            ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
55	            cwd=repo,
56	        )
57	    return worktree_path
58	
59	
60	def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> WorktreeHandle:
61	    """Reuse a preserved worktree if one is already registered, else add.
62	
63	    A worktree registered at ``repo/.worktrees/<identifier>`` is reused
64	    as-is — no git command is run, so a dirty index, staged or untracked
65	    files, and the gitignored config symlinks all survive untouched. Any
66	    other state (no entry at that path) falls through to :func:`add`,
67	    whose ``RuntimeError`` on a leftover branch or orphan directory is
68	    what the orchestrator's existing pre-spawn handler turns into the
69	    clean ``Halt: … — setup failed: …`` line.
70	    """
71	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
72	    if _is_registered_worktree(repo, worktree_path):
73	        return WorktreeHandle(path=worktree_path, resumed=True)
74	    return WorktreeHandle(path=add(repo, identifier), resumed=False)
75	
76	
77	def link_project_config(
78	    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
79	) -> list[Path]:
80	    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the worktree.
81	
82	    A git worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored project config
83	    (``.claude/`` settings/hooks/agents/skills, a root ``.mcp.json``) is absent.
84	    Linking the repo's real entries in gives a worker the same settings, hooks,
85	    agents, skills, and MCP config as an interactive session at the repo root —
86	    and because the link points at the live dir, a stateful hook reads and
87	    writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run would.
88	
89	    For each name: skip it if absent in ``repo`` (a clean no-op for repos
90	    without that config) or if something already occupies that path in the
91	    worktree (a tracked entry git checked out, or a pre-existing link). The
92	    check uses ``os.path.lexists`` so a dangling link counts as present and is
93	    never clobbered. Returns the links created.
94	    """
95	    created: list[Path] = []
96	    repo = repo.resolve()
97	    for name in names:
98	        source = repo / name
99	        if not source.exists():
100	            continue
101	        link = worktree_path / name
102	        if os.path.lexists(link):
103	            continue
104	        os.symlink(source, link)
105	        created.append(link)
106	    return created
107	
108	
109	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
110	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
111	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.remove") as span:
112	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
113	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
114	        _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
115	
116	
117	def _is_registered_worktree(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> bool:
118	    """Return ``True`` if git lists a worktree at ``worktree_path``.
119	
120	    Parses ``git worktree list --porcelain`` for a ``worktree <path>``
121	    record matching ``worktree_path.resolve()``. The resolve step matches
122	    git's own canonicalisation (symlinks, ``..``) so a worktree under a
123	    symlinked repo path still matches the entry git printed.
124	    """
125	    target = worktree_path.resolve()
126	    result = subprocess.run(
127	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain"],
128	        cwd=repo,
129	        check=False,
130	        capture_output=True,
131	        text=True,
132	    )
133	    if result.returncode != 0:
134	        # Treat any failure as "not registered" — the caller then falls
135	        # through to ``add``, whose own error surfaces git's real
136	        # diagnostic via the orchestrator's pre-spawn halt path.
137	        return False
138	    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():
139	        if not line.startswith("worktree "):
140	            continue
141	        listed = Path(line.removeprefix("worktree ").strip())
142	        if listed.resolve() == target:
143	            return True
144	    return False
145	
146	
147	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
148	    result = subprocess.run(
149	        ["git", *args],
150	        cwd=cwd,
151	        check=False,
152	        capture_output=True,
153	        text=True,
154	    )
155	    if result.returncode != 0:
156	        raise RuntimeError(
157	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
158	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
159	        )
160

Good — `resolve()` is non-strict by default, so missing paths still canonicalize. And note the `/tmp` → `/private/tmp` symlink resolution on macOS, which is exactly the symlinked-repo-path case the code handles.

## Review Summary

**Verdict:** APPROVE (with one Important nit worth fixing before merge)

**Overview:** Small, well-scoped PR1 that introduces `ensure()` + `WorktreeHandle` as the reuse seam, leaves `add()` untouched, and pins the no-op-on-reuse contract with tests against real git. The implementation respects the issue's two-branch design ("registered → reuse; everything else → fall through to add"), runs no recovery commands, and surfaces git's real diagnostic via the existing halt path. Tests are tight and assert byte-identical `status`/`log` before and after — strong evidence for the no-op claim.

### Critical Issues
- None.

### Important Issues
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py:138-143` — Porcelain parsing strips the `worktree ` prefix and then calls `.strip()` on the remainder. If a path ever contains a trailing space (legal in POSIX) or, worse, embedded `\n`, this loses information. More importantly, `git worktree list --porcelain` since git 2.48 emits a `worktree-format-version 1` directive that uses the **`-z`** form when paths contain unsafe characters — without `-z`, paths with newlines are not representable and git silently truncates. For the cycle loop today the operator owns the paths (`.worktrees/<identifier>`, identifiers are Linear IDs like `ABA-299`) so this is theoretical, but the fix is one flag: pass `-z` and split on `\0`, or — simpler — sidestep parsing entirely by checking `(worktree_path / ".git").exists()` and confirming with `git -C <wt> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`. The current parser is correct for every identifier this repo will ever generate; flag is for the next reader who copies this code to a context with looser identifiers.

### Suggestions
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py:60-74` — `ensure()` has no telemetry span while `add()` and `remove()` do. A `drain.worktree.ensure` span with a `worktree.resumed` attribute would let Honeycomb show resume rate over time — directly answers "is the resume primitive being exercised in real drains?" Cheap to add and matches the recent OTel commit's intent.
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py:117` — Docstring says "Parses `git worktree list --porcelain` for a `worktree <path>` record" — accurate, but doesn't mention the swallow-nonzero-exit branch. One extra sentence ("A non-zero exit is treated as not-registered so `add()` runs and surfaces git's real error") would put the load-bearing behaviour in the docstring next to the code.
- `tests/test_worktree.py:316-340` — Test name says "link_project_config idempotent on reuse" but the test also asserts `reused.resumed is True`. Two assertions in one test is fine; consider renaming to `test_ensure_reuse_then_link_project_config_is_idempotent` so the intent reads off the name.
- Consider exporting `WorktreeHandle` from `drain_cycle/__init__.py` if PR2/3 will import it; if not, ignore.

### What's Done Well
- The dirty-state test (`test_ensure_reuse_preserves_dirty_state_and_runs_no_git_command`) is the right test: it captures `git status --porcelain` and `git log --oneline` byte-strings before and after and asserts equality. That's exactly the "no git command run" contract from the design doc, expressed as observable evidence rather than as a mock assertion.
- The leftover-branch-without-worktree case is explicitly tested and asserts `RuntimeError` propagation with both `"worktree add"` and the identifier in the message — pins the "no auto-recovery" rule and the orchestrator's halt-line contract in one assertion.
- `add()` is genuinely untouched. The new seam is `ensure()`; existing callers can migrate in PR2/3 without churn.
- `Path.resolve()` on both sides of the comparison correctly handles the symlinked-repo-path case (verified: macOS `/tmp` → `/private/tmp`).

### Design-doc conformance
- "Two branches, not five": yes — `registered → reuse` / `not registered → add`.
- "No auto-recovery of partial states": yes — leftover branch without worktree falls through to `add()` and raises (tested).
- "Reuse runs no git command": yes — the only git call on the reuse path is `git worktree list --porcelain`, which is read-only and observably non-mutating (tested).

### Verification Story
- Tests reviewed: yes — 4 new tests, all asserting observable git state rather than mocks. 12/12 pass locally (`uv run pytest tests/test_worktree.py`).
- Build verified: yes — full worktree test module green.
- Security checked: yes — `subprocess.run` is called with a list (no shell), `cwd=repo` is operator-owned, no untrusted input flows to git. Nothing actionable.

### Relevant files
- /Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py
- /Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/tests/test_worktree.py

  "old_string": "def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> WorktreeHandle:\n    \"\"\"Reuse a preserved worktree if one is already registered, else add.\n\n    A worktree registered at ``repo/.worktrees/<identifier>`` is reused\n    as-is — no git command is run, so a dirty index, staged or untracked\n    files, and the gitignored config symlinks all survive untouched. Any\n    other state (no entry at that path) falls through to :func:`add`,\n    whose ``RuntimeError`` on a leftover branch or orphan directory is\n    what the orchestrator's existing pre-spawn handler turns into the\n    clean ``Halt: … — setup failed: …`` line.\n    \"\"\"\n    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier\n    if _is_registered_worktree(repo, worktree_path):\n        return WorktreeHandle(path=worktree_path, resumed=True)\n    return WorktreeHandle(path=add(repo, identifier), resumed=False)",
  "new_string": "def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> WorktreeHandle:\n    \"\"\"Reuse a preserved worktree if one is already registered, else add.\n\n    A worktree registered at ``repo/.worktrees/<identifier>`` is reused\n    as-is — no mutating git command is run, so a dirty index, staged or\n    untracked files, and the gitignored config symlinks all survive\n    untouched. Any other state (no entry at that path) falls through to\n    :func:`add`, whose ``RuntimeError`` on a leftover branch or orphan\n    directory is what the orchestrator's existing pre-spawn handler\n    turns into the clean ``Halt: … — setup failed: …`` line.\n    \"\"\"\n    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier\n    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span(\"drain.worktree.ensure\") as span:\n        span.set_attribute(\"worktree.identifier\", identifier)\n        span.set_attribute(\"worktree.repo\", repo.name)\n        span.set_attribute(\"worktree.path\", str(worktree_path))\n        if _is_registered_worktree(repo, worktree_path):\n            span.set_attribute(\"worktree.resumed\", True)\n            return WorktreeHandle(path=worktree_path, resumed=True)\n        span.set_attribute(\"worktree.resumed\", False)\n    return WorktreeHandle(path=add(repo, identifier), resumed=False)"
}

The resolve step matches\n    git's own canonicalisation (symlinks, ``..``) so a worktree under a\n    symlinked repo path still matches the entry git printed.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = worktree_path.resolve()\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"worktree\", \"list\", \"--porcelain\"],\n        cwd=repo,\n        check=False,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        # Treat any failure as \"not registered\" — the caller then falls\n        # through to ``add``, whose own error surfaces git's real\n        # diagnostic via the orchestrator's pre-spawn halt path.\n        return False\n    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():\n        if not line.startswith(\"worktree \"):\n            continue\n        listed = Path(line.removeprefix(\"worktree \").strip())\n        if listed.resolve() == target:\n            return True\n    return False",
  "new_string": "def _is_registered_worktree(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return ``True`` if git lists a worktree at ``worktree_path``.\n\n    Parses ``git worktree list --porcelain -z`` for a ``worktree <path>``\n    record matching ``worktree_path.resolve()``. ``-z`` makes each record\n    NUL-separated and each field NUL-terminated, so paths with embedded\n    spaces or newlines are unambiguous. The resolve step matches git's\n    own canonicalisation (symlinks, ``..``) so a worktree under a\n    symlinked repo path still matches the entry git printed. A non-zero\n    git exit is treated as not-registered so ``ensure`` falls through to\n    ``add``, whose error surfaces git's real diagnostic via the\n    orchestrator's pre-spawn halt path.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = worktree_path.resolve()\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"worktree\", \"list\", \"--porcelain\", \"-z\"],\n        cwd=repo,\n        check=False,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        return False\n    for field in result.stdout.split(\"\\0\"):\n        if not field.startswith(\"worktree \"):\n            continue\n        listed = Path(field.removeprefix(\"worktree \"))\n        if listed.resolve() == target:\n            return True\n    return False"

This is PR2 of a 4-PR Graphite stack for Linear issue ABA-299 (\"Resume a halted issue on re-run\").\n\nContext:\n- `drain-cycle` is a single-user CLI that drains a Linear cycle by spawning `claude -p` workers in per-issue git worktrees. The spawned prompt is the whole contract: title + description + 4-segment preamble + a `_TAIL` line. The four-segment ordering is load-bearing — preamble explains the procedure, `_TAIL` is intentionally last because models attend most strongly to trailing tokens.\n- This PR adds a `resumed=False` kwarg to `prompt.build()` so a future orchestrator change can tell the spawned agent \"the worktree carries prior committed work — read it and continue.\"\n\nScope of PR2 (changes in this diff only):\n- `drain_cycle/prompt.py`: new private `_resume_directive(identifier) -> str` returning the directive paragraph; `build()` gains `resumed: bool = False` kwarg and prepends the directive *inside the preamble*, after the `---` separator but before \"Execution instructions:\". `_TAIL` is unchanged and still rendered last.\n- `tests/test_prompt.py`: two new tests — (a) `resumed=True` puts the directive after `---` and before `Execution instructions:` while keeping `_TAIL` as the last non-empty line; (b) `resumed=False` is byte-identical to the no-kwarg default.\n\nWhat I want you to evaluate:\n1. Correctness — does the directive land in the right position? Does `_TAIL` remain the absolute last non-empty line? Are existing call sites still passing the same prompt today (backwards-compat)?\n2. Readability — is the resume text actionable for a Claude agent? Is the function-extraction worth it vs. a single conditional string?\n3. Architecture — is the kwarg the right shape, or should we have a separate `build_resume(...)` function?\n4. Security — none expected; the directive is static text.\n5. Performance — none.\n\nSpecifically watch for:\n- The original four-segment ordering test (`test_prompt_contains_four_segments_in_order`) still passing — confirms backwards-compat.\n- Whether the resume directive could confuse the agent when the preserved worktree is actually empty (zero commits on the issue branch). The issue acknowledges this edge case: \"If a worker dies before committing anything, resume re-runs from an empty branch — no worse than today, and bounded by the cap.\" The directive itself should still be safe in that case because `git log` and `git status` will return clean output.\n\nReport Critical / Required / Suggested findings with line numbers. Under 500 words."
}

Full suite passes (207 tests). Now I have everything I need for the review.

## Review Summary

**Verdict:** APPROVE

**Overview:** A small, well-scoped change that adds an opt-in `resumed=True` kwarg to `prompt.build()`, prepending a resume directive inside the preamble without disturbing the load-bearing four-segment ordering. Tests pin both the new behaviour and byte-identical backwards-compat. Sole existing call site (`orchestrator.py:289`) continues to work unchanged.

### Critical Issues
None.

### Required Issues
None.

### Suggestions

- **`drain_cycle/prompt.py:34`** — The directive instructs the agent to run `git log --oneline main..HEAD`, but the preserved worktree's branch parent isn't guaranteed to be `main` in all repos (this repo happens to use `main`; some repos in the workspace may not). For now this matches the rest of the preamble (`Base branch: main` at line 51), so consistency is preserved — but if PR3/PR4 makes the base branch configurable, both spots need to thread the same value. Worth a note in the design doc rather than a code change now.

- **`drain_cycle/prompt.py:31-37`** — The directive opens with `f"Resuming issue {identifier}: …"`. If `identifier` is ever an empty string (Linear has never returned this in practice but `build()` defensively defaults to `""` on line 43), the output becomes `"Resuming issue : this worktree…"` with an odd double-space-around-colon. Low likelihood; not worth a guard, but worth a journal entry if you ever see it in the wild.

- **`drain_cycle/prompt.py:23`** — The `_resume_directive` helper extraction is justified — it gives the directive a docstring explaining *why* it sits between `---` and "Execution instructions:" (the load-bearing position constraint), which a single inline conditional couldn't carry. Keep it.

- **`tests/test_prompt.py:111-124`** — The byte-identical test is the right shape for backwards-compat, but consider also asserting against a captured golden string (or at least the length) — `default == explicit_false` only proves the two kwarg-paths agree, not that *neither* drifted from the pre-PR rendering. Minor; the existing `test_prompt_contains_four_segments_in_order` covers structural drift.

- **Architecture** — The `resumed=False` kwarg is the right shape over a separate `build_resume()`. The two prompts share ~95% of their text and all four segments; splitting would duplicate the preamble and risk drift between modes. Keyword-only (`*,`) is correct — prevents positional-arg confusion at call sites.

### What's Done Well

- The four-segment ordering invariant is explicitly named in the docstring at `prompt.py:23-30` and pinned by a new test assertion at `test_prompt.py:97` and `:107-108`. The PR is honest about what it's preserving.
- The new test names read like specifications: `test_resumed_prompt_inserts_directive_above_execution_instructions` and `test_unresumed_prompt_is_byte_identical_to_no_kwarg_default` describe the behavioural contract, not the implementation.
- The directive text is actionable for a Claude agent: two specific commands (`git log --oneline main..HEAD`, `git status`) and a clear "do not restart from scratch" anti-instruction. The edge case where the branch is empty is benign — both commands return clean output, and the agent will see there's nothing to resume from.
- Comments adhere to the AGENTS.md no-fix-history rule — no `ABA-299`, no commit SHAs, no "added for…" narration. The docstring at `prompt.py:24-30` explains the invariant ("`_TAIL` still holds the last-line position the four-segment ordering reserves for it") as-it-stands, not as-it-was-fixed.

### Verification Story

- Tests reviewed: yes. Two new tests cover the positive (`resumed=True`) and negative (`resumed=False` byte-identical) cases. Pre-existing `test_prompt_contains_four_segments_in_order` still passes — confirms backwards-compat structurally as well as byte-wise.
- Build verified: yes. Full suite: 207 passed in 17.82s. Prompt-specific: 6 passed.
- Backwards-compat verified: yes. Only existing caller is `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:289` (`prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)`) — unchanged signature path, byte-identical output via the default `resumed=False`.
- Security checked: yes. Static text; `identifier` is interpolated but flows into a prompt string handed to a local subprocess, not a shell or SQL boundary. No new attack surface.

1	"""Per-issue and per-cycle resource guardrails.
2	
3	A spawned ``claude -p`` worker can run away — burning tokens, wall-clock,
4	and quota with no ceiling but the outer per-issue timeout. This module
5	defines the thresholds that bound that spend and the ``Breach`` value that
6	names which one was crossed.
7	
8	**Two layers, belt and suspenders.**
9	
10	* *Native belt* — ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to ``claude`` as
11	  ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost without the
12	  orchestrator having to watch it.
13	* *Orchestrator suspenders* — ``per_issue_tokens`` and ``per_issue_seconds``
14	  are enforced by the worker against the live event stream: when either is
15	  crossed the worker SIGKILLs the session's process group (see
16	  ``worker.py``). The ``cycle_*`` caps are enforced by the orchestrator
17	  between issues via ``check_cycle`` — death-by-aggregate, where every issue
18	  is individually under its cap but their sum drains the quota, is exactly
19	  what the per-issue caps cannot catch.
20	
21	Token count is the primary guardrail (a subscription user pays in tokens,
22	not dollars); cost rides alongside it.
23	
24	**On/off per guardrail.** Any threshold may be ``None`` — that guardrail is
25	off. The defaults below are all live; an operator turns one off by setting
26	its key to ``null`` in ``~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml``.
27	
28	**Load precedence.** Baked-in defaults, overlaid by ``limits.yml`` if it
29	exists: a key absent from the file keeps its default, an explicit ``null``
30	turns the guardrail off, a positive number overrides it. A present-but-
31	malformed file raises ``LimitsConfigError`` rather than silently falling
32	back to defaults — a typo that quietly disabled a cap the operator believed
33	was active is worse than a loud startup halt. The defaults are deliberately
34	generous starting points; recalibrate against real run-log spend.
35	"""
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
39	from pathlib import Path
40	from typing import Any
41	
42	import yaml
43	
44	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml"
45	
46	
47	class LimitsConfigError(RuntimeError):
48	    """``limits.yml`` exists but is malformed."""
49	
50	
51	@dataclass(frozen=True)
52	class Breach:
53	    """A crossed guardrail, recorded into the halting run-log entry.
54	
55	    ``scope`` is ``"per-issue"`` or ``"cycle"``; ``metric`` is ``"token"``,
56	    ``"time"``, or ``"cost"``. ``limit`` is the cap that was set and
57	    ``observed`` the value at the moment of the breach. ``describe`` renders
58	    the operator-facing line — the worker uses it for a per-issue kill and
59	    the orchestrator for a cycle stop, so the wording cannot drift between
60	    the two surfaces.
61	    """
62	
63	    scope: str
64	    metric: str
65	    limit: float
66	    observed: float
67	
68	    def describe(self) -> str:
69	        return f"{self.scope} {self.metric} cap exceeded: {self._detail()}"
70	
71	    def _detail(self) -> str:
72	        if self.metric == "cost":
73	            return f"${self.observed:,.2f} ≥ ${self.limit:,.2f}"
74	        if self.metric == "time":
75	            return f"{self.observed:,.0f}s ≥ {self.limit:,.0f}s"
76	        return f"{self.observed:,.0f} ≥ {self.limit:,.0f} tokens"
77	
78	
79	@dataclass(frozen=True)
80	class Limits:
81	    """Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.
82	
83	    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·
84	    90 min · $60.
85	    """
86	
87	    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000
88	    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60
89	    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0
90	    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000
91	    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60
92	    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0
93	
94	
95	def default_config_path() -> Path:
96	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
97	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "limits.yml"
98	
99	
100	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Limits:
101	    """Read ``limits.yml`` over the baked-in defaults.
102	
103	    A missing or empty file yields the defaults unchanged — ``limits.yml``
104	    is optional. A present file maps limit names to positive numbers (or
105	    ``null`` to disable that guardrail); unknown keys, non-numeric values,
106	    booleans, and non-positive numbers each raise ``LimitsConfigError`` with
107	    a message naming the on-disk problem.
108	    """
109	    defaults = Limits()
110	    if path is None:
111	        path = default_config_path()
112	    if not path.exists():
113	        return defaults
114	    try:
115	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
116	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
117	        raise LimitsConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}") from None
118	    if data is None:
119	        return defaults
120	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
121	        raise LimitsConfigError(
122	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must be a mapping of limit names to numbers"
123	        )
124	
125	    valid = [f.name for f in fields(Limits)]
126	    unknown = set(data) - set(valid)
127	    if unknown:
128	        raise LimitsConfigError(
129	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} has unknown keys: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}; "
130	            f"valid keys: {', '.join(valid)}"
131	        )
132	
133	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
134	    for name in valid:
135	        if name not in data:
136	            values[name] = getattr(defaults, name)
137	            continue
138	        values[name] = _coerce(name, data[name])
139	    return Limits(**values)
140	
141	
142	def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:
143	    """Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number."""
144	    if raw is None:
145	        return None
146	    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would
147	    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.
148	    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:
149	        raise LimitsConfigError(
150	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number "
151	            f"or null (got {raw!r})"
152	        )
153	    return raw
154	
155	
156	def check_cycle(
157	    limits: Limits, *, tokens: int, cost_usd: float, seconds: float
158	) -> Breach | None:
159	    """Return the first cycle-wide cap the running totals breach, else ``None``.
160	
161	    Tokens are checked first (the primary guardrail), then cost, then
162	    wall-clock. A guardrail set to ``None`` is skipped.
163	    """
164	    if limits.cycle_tokens is not None and tokens >= limits.cycle_tokens:
165	        return Breach("cycle", "token", limits.cycle_tokens, tokens)
166	    if limits.cycle_cost_usd is not None and cost_usd >= limits.cycle_cost_usd:
167	        return Breach("cycle", "cost", limits.cycle_cost_usd, cost_usd)
168	    if limits.cycle_seconds is not None and seconds >= limits.cycle_seconds:
169	        return Breach("cycle", "time", limits.cycle_seconds, seconds)
170	    return None
171	
```

1	"""Unit tests for the guardrail thresholds and their loader.
2	
3	These exercise pure logic — no subprocess, no Linear. The breach *kills*
4	are exercised against a real process group in ``test_worker.py`` (per-issue)
5	and through the orchestrator in ``test_orchestrator_cycle_limit.py``
6	(cycle-wide). Here we pin: the defaults, the ``limits.yml`` precedence
7	(absent → default, ``null`` → off, number → override), the malformed-config
8	rejections, the ``check_cycle`` ordering, and the ``Breach`` wording.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	
14	import pytest
15	
16	from drain_cycle import limits
17	
18	
19	def _write(tmp_path: Path, body: str) -> Path:
20	    path = tmp_path / "limits.yml"
21	    path.write_text(body)
22	    return path
23	
24	
25	def test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_absent(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
26	    loaded = limits.load(tmp_path / "nonexistent.yml")
27	    assert loaded == limits.Limits()
28	    # The documented defaults, pinned so a careless edit to the dataclass
29	    # surfaces here.
30	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens == 8_000_000
31	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == 20 * 60
32	    assert loaded.per_issue_cost_usd == 15.0
33	    assert loaded.cycle_tokens == 30_000_000
34	    assert loaded.cycle_seconds == 90 * 60
35	    assert loaded.cycle_cost_usd == 60.0
36	
37	
38	def test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_empty(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
39	    assert limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "")) == limits.Limits()
40	
41	
42	def test_load_overrides_only_named_keys_keeping_other_defaults(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
43	    loaded = limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: 1000000\ncycle_cost_usd: 25\n"))
44	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens == 1_000_000
45	    assert loaded.cycle_cost_usd == 25
46	    # Untouched keys keep their baked-in defaults.
47	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == limits.Limits().per_issue_seconds
48	    assert loaded.cycle_tokens == limits.Limits().cycle_tokens
49	
50	
51	def test_load_null_value_turns_a_guardrail_off(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
52	    loaded = limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: null\nper_issue_cost_usd: null\n"))
53	    assert loaded.per_issue_tokens is None
54	    assert loaded.per_issue_cost_usd is None
55	    # A sibling guardrail stays at its default — "off" is per-key.
56	    assert loaded.per_issue_seconds == limits.Limits().per_issue_seconds
57	
58	
59	def test_load_rejects_non_mapping(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
60	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="mapping of limit names"):
61	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "- just\n- a\n- list\n"))
62	
63	
64	def test_load_rejects_unknown_key(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
65	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="unknown keys: per_issue_dollars"):
66	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_dollars: 5\n"))
67	
68	
69	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
70	    "value",
71	    ["0", "-1", "abc", "true"],
72	)
73	def test_load_rejects_non_positive_or_non_numeric(tmp_path: Path, value: str) -> None:
74	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="positive number or null"):
75	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, f"per_issue_tokens: {value}\n"))
76	
77	
78	def test_load_rejects_invalid_yaml(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
79	    with pytest.raises(limits.LimitsConfigError, match="not valid YAML"):
80	        limits.load(_write(tmp_path, "per_issue_tokens: [unclosed\n"))
81	
82	
83	def test_check_cycle_no_breach_under_all_caps() -> None:
84	    lim = limits.Limits()
85	    assert limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=1, cost_usd=0.5, seconds=10.0) is None
86	
87	
88	def test_check_cycle_tokens_take_precedence_as_primary_guardrail() -> None:
89	    # Both tokens and cost are over their caps; tokens is reported first.
90	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=100, cycle_cost_usd=10.0)
91	    breach = limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=200, cost_usd=50.0, seconds=0.0)
92	    assert breach is not None
93	    assert breach.scope == "cycle"
94	    assert breach.metric == "token"
95	    assert breach.limit == 100
96	    assert breach.observed == 200
97	
98	
99	def test_check_cycle_reports_cost_then_time() -> None:
100	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=10.0, cycle_seconds=5.0)
101	    cost_breach = limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=999, cost_usd=12.0, seconds=99.0)
102	    assert cost_breach is not None and cost_breach.metric == "cost"
103	
104	    lim_time_only = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=None, cycle_seconds=5.0)
105	    time_breach = limits.check_cycle(lim_time_only, tokens=999, cost_usd=999.0, seconds=6.0)
106	    assert time_breach is not None and time_breach.metric == "time"
107	
108	
109	def test_check_cycle_skips_disabled_guardrails() -> None:
110	    lim = limits.Limits(cycle_tokens=None, cycle_cost_usd=None, cycle_seconds=None)
111	    assert limits.check_cycle(lim, tokens=10**12, cost_usd=10**6, seconds=10**6) is None
112	
113	
114	def test_breach_describe_wording() -> None:
115	    assert (
116	        limits.Breach("per-issue", "token", 8_000_000, 9_000_000).describe()
117	        == "per-issue token cap exceeded: 9,000,000 ≥ 8,000,000 tokens"
118	    )
119	    assert (
120	        limits.Breach("cycle", "cost", 60.0, 62.5).describe()
121	        == "cycle cost cap exceeded: $62.50 ≥ $60.00"
122	    )
123	    assert (
124	        limits.Breach("per-issue", "time", 1200, 1201).describe()
125	        == "per-issue time cap exceeded: 1,201s ≥ 1,200s"
126	    )
127

id: toolu_01VbYWg1qWKYVMijue8fRv5F
```
## Review Summary

**Verdict:** APPROVE with one Required nit.

**Overview:** A clean, narrow knob-only PR. The new field plugs into the existing `_coerce` validator without code changes, the docstring update is honest, and the four new tests mirror the contract of the surrounding guardrail keys. Type choice (`int | None`) matches the other count-like fields. Architectural home is right.

### Critical
- None.

### Required
- `tests/test_limits.py:103` — the parametrised rejection test does not cover the `1.5` / float case, but `_coerce` will happily accept a float and store it on a field typed `int | None`. PR4 will use this value as an attempt count; `range(int(cap))` or comparison against `len(prior_attempts)` is fine, but `1.5` is a footgun the operator would not expect to be silently accepted on a count field. Either add a parametrised case asserting `1.5` is rejected (matching the `int | None` typing) and tighten `_coerce` to reject non-integer floats for `*_tokens` and `max_resume_attempts`, or document explicitly that floats round/truncate. The cheapest fix: add `"1.5"` to the parametrise list and gate it in `_coerce` by field name. Current PR scope arguably permits deferring to PR4, but the contract this PR ships is the place to lock it.

### Suggestions
- `drain_cycle/limits.py:81` — the class docstring says `None` on any field turns "that guardrail off" — but `max_resume_attempts` is not really a guardrail in the same sense as the token/time/cost caps; it's a respawn policy. The new sentence is honest, but consider a one-line note that this is a *policy* cap, not a runtime breach, so readers don't grep for a `Breach("…", "resume", …)` that doesn't exist. Doesn't justify a separate `Policy` object yet — adding one for one field would be over-abstracted; revisit if a second policy knob appears.
- `tests/test_limits.py:84` — naming nit: `test_load_max_resume_attempts_absent_keeps_default` duplicates coverage already implicit in `test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_absent` + `test_load_overrides_only_named_keys_keeping_other_defaults`. Not load-bearing; the explicit test reads well as documentation, so fine to keep.

### Nits
- The docstring phrase "loop indefinitely across re-runs" is slightly dramatic — `None` doesn't cause a loop, it just refuses to short-circuit one. "removes the cap so a perma-stuck issue keeps being retried on every re-run" is more accurate.

### What's done well
- Pinning the new default in `test_load_returns_defaults_when_file_absent` alongside the other six defaults is exactly right — a careless dataclass edit surfaces in one place.
- The `true → 1` rationalisation is pre-rebutted in the test docstring, matching the same defense already in `_coerce`'s comment. Future PR4 author can't accidentally arm a one-shot cap.
- Zero production code beyond the dataclass field — the validator generalises for free. This is the minimum-viable knob.
- Scope discipline: no PR4 wiring leaks in.

### Verification story
- Tests reviewed: yes — 4 new + 1 pinned default, all asserting via the public `load()` API rather than poking the dataclass directly.
- Build verified: not run (review-only); diff is additive and the validator already handles the new field.
- Security checked: yes — no new input surface beyond the existing YAML loader, which already rejects unknown keys, non-mappings, booleans, and non-positive numbers.

### Files reviewed
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/limits.py`
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/tests/test_limits.py`

  "new_string": "@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Limits:\n    \"\"\"Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.\n\n    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·\n    90 min · $60.\n\n    ``max_resume_attempts`` is a *policy* cap, not a runtime guardrail:\n    it bounds how many times the orchestrator will spawn a worker for a\n    halted issue across re-runs before refusing further attempts\n    (default 3, integer). ``None`` removes the cap so a perma-stuck\n    issue keeps being retried on every re-run. No ``Breach`` is raised\n    when it fires — the cap is checked pre-spawn against the run-log\n    history.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000\n    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60\n    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0\n    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000\n    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60\n    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0\n    max_resume_attempts: int | None = 3\n\n\n_INT_ONLY_FIELDS = frozenset({\"max_resume_attempts\"})"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/limits.py",
  "old_string": "def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:\n    \"\"\"Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number.\"\"\"\n    if raw is None:\n        return None\n    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would\n    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.\n    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:\n        raise LimitsConfigError(\n            f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number \"\n            f\"or null (got {raw!r})\"\n        )\n    return raw",
  "new_string": "def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:\n    \"\"\"Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number.\n\n    Fields in :data:`_INT_ONLY_FIELDS` reject non-integer floats — a count\n    like ``max_resume_attempts: 1.5`` is semantically incoherent and would\n    otherwise be stored verbatim against an ``int | None`` field.\n    \"\"\"\n    if raw is None:\n        return None\n    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would\n    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.\n    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:\n        raise LimitsConfigError(\n            f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number \"\n            f\"or null (got {raw!r})\"\n        )\n    if name in _INT_ONLY_FIELDS and not isinstance(raw, int):\n        raise LimitsConfigError(\n            f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive integer \"\n            f\"or null (got {raw!r})\"\n        )\n    return raw"
}
