1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import os
12	import sys
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	from opentelemetry.trace import Span
17	
18	from . import linear, model, progress, prompt, runlog, telemetry, worker, worktree
19	from .limits import Limits, check_cycle
20	from .linear import DependencyCycleError
21	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
22	
23	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
24	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
25	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
26	_DEBUG_ENV_VAR = "DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG"
27	"""Opt-in switch for per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture. Any non-empty value
28	turns it on; the worker then writes each session's startup diagnostics
29	(settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, hooks) beside the run log. Off by
30	default — the diagnostic exists for one-shot investigation, not steady state.
31	See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10."""
32	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
33	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
34	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
35	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
36	rather than a misleading fake path."""
37	
38	
39	def _now_iso() -> str:
40	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
41	
42	
43	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
44	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
45	
46	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
47	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
48	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
49	    time.
50	    """
51	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
52	
53	
54	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
55	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
56	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
57	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
58	
59	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
60	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
61	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
62	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
63	
64	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
65	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
66	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
67	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
68	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
69	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
70	    """
71	    try:
72	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
73	    except Exception as exc:
74	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
75	    try:
76	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
77	    except Exception:
78	        return target_state_name, None
79	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
80	
81	
82	def _worker_log_fields(result: worker.WorkerResult) -> dict[str, object]:
83	    """Map a ``WorkerResult`` onto the run-log entry's usage fields.
84	
85	    Shared by all three worker-backed ``append_entry`` calls (timeout
86	    halt, Done, not-Done halt) so the recorded usage shape can't drift
87	    between branches.
88	    """
89	    return {
90	        "duration_seconds": result.duration_seconds,
91	        "model": result.model,
92	        "usage": result.usage,
93	        "cost_usd": result.cost_usd,
94	        "num_turns": result.num_turns,
95	        "session_id": result.session_id,
96	        "is_error": result.is_error,
97	    }
98	
99	
100	def _debug_enabled() -> bool:
101	    """Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.
102	
103	    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any
104	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator
105	    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1
106	    drain-cycle`` and leaves it off otherwise.
107	    """
108	    return bool(os.environ.get(_DEBUG_ENV_VAR))
109	
110	
111	def run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits | None = None) -> int:
112	    """Drain the current cycle inside the ``drain.cycle`` root span.
113	
114	    The span wrapper is thin so the body keeps its shape; per-issue work nests
115	    under it via ``_drain_one_issue``'s ``drain.issue`` spans, and the Linear,
116	    worktree, and worker spans nest under those — yielding one trace per drain.
117	    """
118	    if limits is None:
119	        limits = Limits()
120	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.cycle") as cycle_span:
121	        return _run(repos, limits, cycle_span)
122	
123	
124	def _run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits, cycle_span: Span) -> int:
125	    debug = _debug_enabled()
126	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
127	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.cycle_id", cycle_id)
128	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
129	    try:
130	        plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
131	    except DependencyCycleError as exc:
132	        halt_reason = f"Halt: {exc}"
133	        log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
134	        telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-dependency-cycle", halt_reason)
135	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
136	        return 1
137	
138	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_planned", len(plan.order))
139	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_deferred", len(plan.deferred))
140	
141	    if not plan.order and not plan.deferred:
142	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "nothing-to-do")
143	        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
144	        return 0
145	
146	    for deferred in plan.deferred:
147	        issue = deferred["issue"]
148	        blocker_id = deferred["blocker_identifier"]
149	        blocker_state = deferred["blocker_state_type"]
150	        print(
151	            f"drain-cycle: deferred {issue['identifier']}"
152	            f" — blocked by {blocker_id} ({blocker_state})",
153	            file=sys.stderr,
154	        )
155	
156	    if not plan.order:
157	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "all-deferred")
158	        return 0
159	
160	    total = len(plan.order)
161	    for index, issue in enumerate(plan.order):
162	        halt_code = _drain_one_issue(
163	            issue,
164	            index=index,
165	            total=total,
166	            repos=repos,
167	            limits=limits,
168	            log=log,
169	            cycle_id=cycle_id,
170	            debug=debug,
171	        )
172	        if halt_code is not None:
173	            return halt_code
174	
175	        # Cycle-wide circuit breaker: every issue may stay under its own
176	        # per-issue caps while their sum drains the quota. Check the
177	        # running totals (which now include the Done issue just finished)
178	        # before spawning the next one; on breach, stop the cycle.
179	        cycle_breach = check_cycle(
180	            limits,
181	            tokens=log.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
182	            cost_usd=log.cycle_cost_usd(),
183	            seconds=log.cycle_duration_seconds(),
184	        )
185	        if cycle_breach is not None:
186	            halt_reason = f"Halt: {cycle_breach.describe()}"
187	            log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
188	            telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-cycle-breach", halt_reason)
189	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
190	            return 1
191	
192	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "drained")
193	    return 0
194	
195	
196	def _drain_one_issue(
197	    issue: dict,
198	    *,
199	    index: int,
200	    total: int,
201	    repos: Repos,
202	    limits: Limits,
203	    log: runlog.RunLog,
204	    cycle_id: str,
205	    debug: bool,
206	) -> int | None:
207	    """Drain a single issue end to end inside a ``drain.issue`` span.
208	
209	    Returns ``None`` when the issue reached Done (the caller then runs the
210	    cycle-wide circuit breaker and proceeds to the next issue), or an exit
211	    code when the run must halt: repo-resolution failure, pre-spawn setup
212	    failure, a per-issue cap breach, or a session that finished not-Done.
213	    Each halt path is also marked on the span with a static ``exception.slug``.
214	    """
215	    identifier = issue["identifier"]
216	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.issue") as issue_span:
217	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.identifier", identifier)
218	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.title", issue["title"])
219	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.index", index + 1)
220	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.total", total)
221	        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
222	
223	        started_at = _now_iso()
224	        try:
225	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
226	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
227	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
228	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
229	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
230	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
231	            halt_reason = (
232	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
233	                f" — {exc}"
234	            )
235	            log.append_entry(
236	                issue_identifier=identifier,
237	                started_at=started_at,
238	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
239	                exit_code=-1,
240	                final_linear_state=state_name,
241	                REDACTED,
242	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
243	            )
244	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
245	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-repo-resolution", halt_reason)
246	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
247	            return 1
248	
249	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.repo", target_repo.name)
250	
251	        try:
252	            worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
253	            # A worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored
254	            # project config (.claude/, .mcp.json) is absent. Symlink it in
255	            # so the worker loads the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP
256	            # as an interactive session at the repo root.
257	            worktree.link_project_config(
258	                target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
259	            )
260	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
261	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
262	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
263	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
264	        except Exception as exc:
265	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
266	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
267	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
268	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
269	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
270	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
271	            halt_reason = (
272	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
273	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
274	            )
275	            log.append_entry(
276	                issue_identifier=identifier,
277	                started_at=started_at,
278	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
279	                exit_code=-1,
280	                final_linear_state=state_name,
281	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
282	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
283	            )
284	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", state_name)
285	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-setup-failed", halt_reason)
286	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
287	            return 1
288	
289	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)
290	        worker_model = model.resolve(issue)
291	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.model", worker_model)
292	
293	        debug_file = log.debug_path(identifier) if debug else None
294	        if debug_file is not None:
295	            print(
296	                f"drain-cycle: {identifier} debug capture → {debug_file}",
297	                file=sys.stderr,
298	            )
299	
300	        marker: dict = {
301	            "pid": os.getpid(),
302	            "cycle_id": cycle_id,
303	            "run_log_path": str(log.path),
304	            "issue": {
305	                "identifier": identifier,
306	                "title": issue["title"],
307	                "repo": target_repo.name,
308	                "worktree_path": str(worktree_path),
309	            },
310	            "model": worker_model,
311	            "started_at": started_at,
312	            "index": index + 1,
313	            "total": total,
314	            "progress": {},
315	        }
316	        progress.write(marker)
317	
318	        def _make_on_progress(m: dict, ident: str):
319	            def _cb(
320	                turns: int,
321	                cumulative_tokens: int,
322	                peak_context_tokens: int,
323	                cost_usd: float | None,
324	                elapsed_seconds: float,
325	            ) -> None:
326	                m["progress"] = {
327	                    "turns": turns,
328	                    "cumulative_tokens": cumulative_tokens,
329	                    "peak_context_tokens": peak_context_tokens,
330	                    "cost_usd": cost_usd,
331	                    "elapsed_seconds": elapsed_seconds,
332	                    "last_event_at": _now_iso(),
333	                }
334	                progress.write(m)
335	                print(
336	                    progress.format_progress_line(
337	                        ident,
338	                        turns,
339	                        cumulative_tokens,
340	                        peak_context_tokens,
341	                        cost_usd,
342	                        elapsed_seconds,
343	                    ),
344	                    file=sys.stderr,
345	                )
346	            return _cb
347	
348	        try:
349	            result = worker.run_issue(
350	                claude_cmd=_CLAUDE_CMD,
351	                model=worker_model,
352	                prompt=agent_prompt,
353	                cwd=worktree_path,
354	                token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
355	                time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
356	                cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
357	                debug_file=debug_file,
358	                on_progress=_make_on_progress(marker, identifier),
359	            )
360	        finally:
361	            progress.clear()
362	        finished_at = _now_iso()
363	
364	        if result.breach is not None:
365	            # The worker crossed a per-issue cap (tokens or time) and was
366	            # process-group killed (grandchildren reaped). Same revert + halt
367	            # contract as a not-Done exit, with the recorded usage of the
368	            # killed session and the breached cap named in the halt reason.
369	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
370	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
371	                issue["id"],
372	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
373	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
374	            )
375	            halt_reason = (
376	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
377	                f" — {result.breach.describe()}"
378	            )
379	            if revert_error is not None:
380	                halt_reason += (
381	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
382	                )
383	            log.append_entry(
384	                issue_identifier=identifier,
385	                started_at=started_at,
386	                finished_at=finished_at,
387	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
388	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
389	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
390	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
391	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
392	            )
393	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
394	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
395	            telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-per-issue-breach", halt_reason)
396	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
397	            return 1
398	
399	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
400	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
401	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
402	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.exit_code", result.exit_code)
403	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.is_done", is_done)
404	
405	        if is_done:
406	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", post_spawn_state)
407	            remove_error: str | None = None
408	            try:
409	                worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
410	            except RuntimeError as exc:
411	                remove_error = str(exc)
412	                issue_span.set_attribute("worktree.remove_error", remove_error)
413	                print(
414	                    f"drain-cycle: {identifier}: worktree teardown failed: {exc}",
415	                    file=sys.stderr,
416	                )
417	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
418	            log.append_entry(
419	                issue_identifier=identifier,
420	                started_at=started_at,
421	                finished_at=finished_at,
422	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
423	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
424	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
425	                halt_reason=remove_error,
426	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
427	            )
428	            if remove_error is None:
429	                print(f"drain-cycle: {identifier} done; worktree removed.", file=sys.stderr)
430	            return None
431	
432	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
433	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
434	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
435	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
436	            issue["id"],
437	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
438	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
439	        )
440	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
441	        if revert_error is not None:
442	            halt_reason += (
443	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
444	            )
445	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
446	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
447	        log.append_entry(
448	            issue_identifier=identifier,
449	            started_at=started_at,
450	            finished_at=finished_at,
451	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
452	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
453	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
454	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
455	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
456	        )
457	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
458	        telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-issue-not-done", halt_reason)
459	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
460	        return 1

1	"""Per-cycle run-log artefact.
2	
3	Each ``drain-cycle`` invocation produces a single JSON file at
4	``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` capturing one
5	entry per attempted issue. The filename embeds the run-start timestamp
6	(UTC, ``%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ``) so re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the
7	same cycle writes a new file instead of clobbering the prior one.
8	Downstream consumers glob the directory and group by ``cycle_id``
9	(carried inside each file).
10	
11	* KR1 (completion %) is computed from ``entries[].final_linear_state``
12	  merged across every file sharing the same ``cycle_id``.
13	* Cycle-drain self-grading reads every file across cycles.
14	* The kill condition (KR1 < 50%) merges per-cycle then thresholds.
15	
16	Schema:
17	
18	::
19	
20	    {
21	      "cycle_id":               "<linear-cycle-uuid>",
22	      "cycle_duration_seconds": <float>,
23	      "cycle_cost_usd":         <float>,
24	      "cycle_tokens_cumulative": <int>,
25	      "cycle_halt_reason":      null | "<cycle-budget halt line>",
26	      "entries":                [
27	        {
28	          "issue_identifier":   "ABA-NNN",
29	          "started_at":         "<iso-8601 UTC>",
30	          "finished_at":        "<iso-8601 UTC>",
31	          "exit_code":          <int>,
32	          "final_linear_state": "Done" | "Todo" | ...,
33	          "worktree_path":      "<absolute path>",
34	          "halt_reason":        null | "<orchestrator stderr halt line>",
35	          "duration_seconds":   <float>,
36	          "model":              null | "<resolved model id>",
37	          "usage":              null | {
38	            "input_tokens":               <int>,
39	            "output_tokens":              <int>,
40	            "cache_creation_input_tokens": <int>,
41	            "cache_read_input_tokens":    <int>,
42	            "cumulative":                 <int>,
43	            "peak_context":               <int>,
44	          },
45	          "cost_usd":           null | <float>,
46	          "num_turns":          null | <int>,
47	          "session_id":         null | "<uuid>",
48	          "is_error":           null | <bool>,
49	        },
50	        ...
51	      ],
52	    }
53	
54	``halt_reason`` is ``null`` on Done entries unless worktree teardown
55	failed after the session completed — in that case the Done entry carries
56	the teardown error string and the drain continues. On the orchestrator's
57	halt entry it is the exact string also written to stderr — both surfaces
58	are produced by the same ``_halt_message`` helper in ``orchestrator.py``
59	so the on-disk and terminal values cannot drift. Non-last entries are
60	``null`` by construction: the orchestrator returns on first halt, so
61	anything before the halt is a Done entry.
62	
63	The worker-usage fields (``model`` through ``is_error``) are populated
64	from the spawned session's stream-json events — see ``worker.py``.
65	Entries written before any session runs (pre-spawn resolution and
66	setup-failure halts) carry ``null`` for ``model`` / ``usage`` /
67	``cost_usd`` / ``num_turns`` / ``session_id`` / ``is_error``; every entry
68	keeps the same key set. ``usage.cumulative`` is the billed-token total
69	across turns; ``usage.peak_context`` is the largest single-turn context.
70	These fields are additive — ``grade.py`` reads only ``cycle_id`` and
71	``entries[].final_linear_state`` / ``exit_code``, so existing run logs
72	without them grade unchanged.
73	
74	``cycle_cost_usd`` and ``cycle_tokens_cumulative`` are per-invocation
75	totals over ``entries`` (entries with ``null`` cost or usage contribute
76	zero), recomputed on every persist alongside ``cycle_duration_seconds``.
77	
78	``cycle_halt_reason`` is ``null`` unless the orchestrator stopped the run
79	because a *cycle-wide* cap (tokens / cost / wall-clock) was breached — the
80	death-by-aggregate case where every issue stayed under its own per-issue
81	caps but their sum crossed the cycle budget. The breaching issue's own
82	entry is recorded normally (it ran to Done); this top-level field is the
83	record of why no further issue was attempted, carrying the same ``Halt:``
84	string printed to stderr. A per-issue breach, by contrast, lands in the
85	halting entry's ``halt_reason`` like any other halt.
86	
87	``cycle_duration_seconds`` is computed automatically on every persist as
88	``max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`` across ``entries`` (``0.0`` when
89	empty). This is the KR2 grading proxy — "how long was the agent doing
90	execution for me unattended" stands in for "operator hands-off time" —
91	and replaces the originally specified ``time_spent`` block, which
92	required manual self-report at cycle close and was never going to be
93	filled in.
94	
95	Persistence is incremental: ``append_entry`` re-serialises the whole dict
96	on every call, so a mid-run crash still leaves a well-formed file with
97	every issue attempted so far. Cycle scale (≤ 15 issues) makes the cost
98	irrelevant.
99	"""
100	from __future__ import annotations
101	
102	import json
103	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
104	from datetime import datetime, timezone
105	from pathlib import Path
106	from typing import Any
107	
108	
109	def runs_dir() -> Path:
110	    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written.
111	
112	    Resolved on every call (not module-import time) so tests can redirect
113	    via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`` after the module is imported.
114	    """
115	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
116	
117	
118	@dataclass
119	class RunLog:
120	    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start.
121	
122	    Writing the initial ``{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds: 0.0,
123	    entries: []}`` shell in ``__post_init__`` means that even a zero-issue
124	    cycle (or a crash before the first ``append_entry``) leaves the
125	    artefact on disk, which is what kill-condition tooling needs to
126	    distinguish "drained nothing" from "never ran".
127	
128	    The filename embeds a UTC run-start timestamp with microsecond
129	    resolution, so two ``RunLog`` instances on the same ``cycle_id``
130	    (re-running ``drain-cycle`` after a halt) write to two separate
131	    files instead of one clobbering the other.
132	    """
133	
134	    cycle_id: str
135	    path: Path = field(init=False)
136	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
137	    cycle_halt_reason: str | None = field(default=None, init=False)
138	
139	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
140	        directory = runs_dir()
141	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
142	        run_started_at = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)
143	        timestamp = run_started_at.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ")
144	        self.path = directory / f"{self.cycle_id}-{timestamp}.json"
145	        self._persist()
146	
147	    def append_entry(
148	        self,
149	        *,
150	        issue_identifier: str,
151	        started_at: str,
152	        finished_at: str,
153	        exit_code: int,
154	        final_linear_state: str,
155	        worktree_path: str,
156	        halt_reason: str | None = None,
157	        duration_seconds: float | None = None,
158	        model: str | None = None,
159	        usage: dict[str, int] | None = None,
160	        cost_usd: float | None = None,
161	        num_turns: int | None = None,
162	        session_id: str | None = None,
163	        is_error: bool | None = None,
164	    ) -> None:
165	        if duration_seconds is None:
166	            duration_seconds = (
167	                datetime.fromisoformat(finished_at)
168	                - datetime.fromisoformat(started_at)
169	            ).total_seconds()
170	        self.entries.append(
171	            {
172	                "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
173	                "started_at": started_at,
174	                "finished_at": finished_at,
175	                "exit_code": exit_code,
176	                "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
177	                "worktree_path": worktree_path,
178	                "halt_reason": halt_reason,
179	                "duration_seconds": duration_seconds,
180	                "model": model,
181	                "usage": usage,
182	                "cost_usd": cost_usd,
183	                "num_turns": num_turns,
184	                "session_id": session_id,
185	                "is_error": is_error,
186	            }
187	        )
188	        self._persist()
189	
190	    def debug_path(self, issue_identifier: str) -> Path:
191	        """Path for one issue's opt-in ``--debug-file`` capture, beside the log.
192	
193	        Named ``<run-log-stem>-<issue-identifier>.debug.log`` so each issue's
194	        startup diagnostics sit next to the run log they belong to and the
195	        run-start timestamp keeps re-runs from clobbering a prior capture.
196	        Only written when debug capture is enabled — see ``orchestrator.run``
197	        and ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10.
198	        """
199	        return self.path.with_name(f"{self.path.stem}-{issue_identifier}.debug.log")
200	
201	    def set_cycle_halt(self, reason: str) -> None:
202	        """Record why a cycle-wide cap stopped the run, and persist.
203	
204	        Called by the orchestrator after the breaching issue's own entry is
205	        already written, so the file carries both the issue's normal entry
206	        and the cycle-level reason no further issue was attempted.
207	        """
208	        self.cycle_halt_reason = reason
209	        self._persist()
210	
211	    def cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
212	        if not self.entries:
213	            return 0.0
214	        starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
215	        finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
216	        return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
217	
218	    def cycle_cost_usd(self) -> float:
219	        return sum(
220	            e["cost_usd"] for e in self.entries if e.get("cost_usd") is not None
221	        )
222	
223	    def cycle_tokens_cumulative(self) -> int:
224	        return sum(
225	            e["usage"]["cumulative"]
226	            for e in self.entries
227	            if e.get("usage") is not None
228	        )
229	
230	    def _persist(self) -> None:
231	        payload = {
232	            "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
233	            "cycle_duration_seconds": self.cycle_duration_seconds(),
234	            "cycle_cost_usd": self.cycle_cost_usd(),
235	            "cycle_tokens_cumulative": self.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
236	            "cycle_halt_reason": self.cycle_halt_reason,
237	            "entries": self.entries,
238	        }
239	        self.path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
240	
```

1	"""Halt-on-not-Done test for the orchestrator.
2	
3	The iteration loop has a minimal non-Done branch that returns ``1`` without
4	removing the worktree. This test pins that contract:
5	if the spawned session exits without flipping the issue to Done, the
6	orchestrator must (a) exit non-zero, (b) leave the worktree on disk so the
7	operator can inspect it, and (c) not touch any subsequent issue.
8	
9	The second test in this file pins the halt-path slice of the run-log
10	artefact: it must contain exactly one entry for the halted issue, with
11	`final_linear_state` reflecting the non-Done state the agent left it in
12	— so KR1 grading sees a halted attempt rather than a missing one.
13	
14	Substitution choices mirror ``test_orchestrator_iteration.py``: real git
15	repo, in-process Linear stub via attribute monkey-patching, fake ``claude``
16	shell script as ``_CLAUDE_CMD``. The difference is the script: here it
17	exits without writing the marker file, so the stubbed ``get_issue`` keeps
18	returning the original Todo state — which is exactly the production
19	failure mode this test guards against.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import json
24	import subprocess
25	from datetime import datetime
26	from pathlib import Path
27	
28	import pytest
29	
30	from drain_cycle import limits, linear, orchestrator, repos
31	
32	
33	_TEST_REPO_NAME = "test-repo"
34	
35	
36	def _issue(
37	    identifier: str,
38	    sort_order: float,
39	    *,
40	    repo_name: str = _TEST_REPO_NAME,
41	) -> dict:
42	    return {
43	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
44	        "identifier": identifier,
45	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
46	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
47	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
48	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
49	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
50	    }
51	
52	
53	def _stub_repos(repo_path: Path) -> repos.Repos:
54	    return repos.Repos(mapping={_TEST_REPO_NAME: repo_path})
55	
56	
57	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
58	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
59	    subprocess.run(
60	        ["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True
61	    )
62	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
63	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
64	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
65	    subprocess.run(
66	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
67	    )
68	
69	
70	def test_orchestrator_halts_when_spawn_leaves_issue_not_done(
71	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
72	) -> None:
73	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
74	    repo.mkdir()
75	    _init_repo(repo)
76	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
77	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
78	
79	    # Two issues; the first will be picked, the spawn will exit without
80	    # marking it Done. The second must remain untouched.
81	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
82	    second = _issue("ABA-SECOND", sort_order=2.0)
83	    raw_issues = [first, second]
84	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
85	
86	    get_issue_calls: list[str] = []
87	
88	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
89	        return "stub-cycle"
90	
91	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
92	        return linear._plan(raw_issues)
93	
94	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
95	        # The fake claude script never flips state, so this always returns
96	        # the original Todo issue. Recording the calls lets us prove the
97	        # orchestrator stopped after the first re-fetch.
98	        get_issue_calls.append(issue_id)
99	        return issues_by_id[issue_id]
100	
101	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
102	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
103	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
104	    # set_state is exercised by tests/test_orchestrator_set_state.py; here it's
105	    # a no-op so this test stays focused on halt behaviour.
106	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
107	
108	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path)
109	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
110	
111	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
112	
113	    assert exit_code != 0
114	    # Loop bailed before touching the second issue. The first id may be
115	    # re-fetched more than once (initial refresh + post-revert refresh);
116	    # the load-bearing invariant is the second issue is absent.
117	    assert get_issue_calls
118	    assert second["id"] not in get_issue_calls
119	    # Second issue's stub state is unchanged.
120	    assert second["state"] == {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"}
121	    # First issue's worktree is still on disk for the operator to inspect.
122	    first_worktree = repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"]
123	    assert first_worktree.is_dir()
124	    # Second issue's worktree was never created.
125	    second_worktree = repo / ".worktrees" / second["identifier"]
126	    assert not second_worktree.exists()
127	
128	    # Spec'd halt UX: a single line on stderr starting with
129	    # `Halt: ` carrying issue identifier, final state name, and the absolute
130	    # worktree path the operator must `cd` to. The `Halt:` token is the
131	    # unique grep anchor — no other stderr line on either branch starts
132	    # with it.
133	    stderr_lines = capsys.readouterr().err.splitlines()
134	    halt_lines = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
135	    assert len(halt_lines) == 1
136	    (halt_line,) = halt_lines
137	    assert first["identifier"] in halt_line
138	    assert first["state"]["name"] in halt_line
139	    assert str(first_worktree) in halt_line
140	
141	
142	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:
143	    """No-op stand-in for ``claude -p`` that exits cleanly without doing work.
144	
145	    The point of the test is the orchestrator's response when a spawn
146	    *succeeds at the process level* but leaves the issue in its original
147	    state — the most pernicious failure mode, because there is no
148	    non-zero exit code to alert on.
149	    """
150	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
151	    script.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nexit 0\n")
152	    script.chmod(0o755)
153	    return script
154	
155	
156	def test_orchestrator_runlog_records_halted_issue_with_non_done_state(
157	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
158	) -> None:
159	    """Halt-path slice of the run-log artefact.
160	
161	    The run-log file must contain exactly one entry — for the halted
162	    issue — with `final_linear_state` matching the non-Done state name
163	    the agent left it in. The second issue must be absent from entries
164	    (never attempted) and absent from disk (worktree never created).
165	    The halted issue's worktree must remain preserved.
166	    """
167	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
168	    repo.mkdir()
169	    _init_repo(repo)
170	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
171	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
172	
173	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
174	    second = _issue("ABA-SECOND", sort_order=2.0)
175	    raw_issues = [first, second]
176	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
177	
178	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
179	        return "stub-cycle-id"
180	
181	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
182	        return linear._plan(raw_issues)
183	
184	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
185	        return issues_by_id[issue_id]
186	
187	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
188	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
189	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
190	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
191	
192	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path)
193	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
194	
195	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
196	    assert exit_code != 0
197	
198	    # Per-run filename: one file per drain-cycle invocation,
199	    # ``<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` — glob to locate it.
200	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
201	    log_files = list(runs_dir.glob("stub-cycle-id-*.json"))
202	    assert len(log_files) == 1
203	    log_path = log_files[0]
204	    payload = json.loads(log_path.read_text())
205	
206	    assert payload["cycle_id"] == "stub-cycle-id"
207	    assert len(payload["entries"]) == 1
208	
209	    # Halt entry's wall-clock duration is the cycle's duration — KR2 still
210	    # accrues the unattended time we spent on the doomed issue.
211	    only_entry = payload["entries"][0]
212	    expected_duration = (
213	        datetime.fromisoformat(only_entry["finished_at"])
214	        - datetime.fromisoformat(only_entry["started_at"])
215	    ).total_seconds()
216	    assert payload["cycle_duration_seconds"] == pytest.approx(expected_duration)
217	    assert payload["cycle_duration_seconds"] >= 0.0
218	
219	    entry = payload["entries"][0]
220	    required_keys = {
221	        "issue_identifier",
222	        "started_at",
223	        "finished_at",
224	        "exit_code",
225	        "final_linear_state",
226	        "worktree_path",
227	        "halt_reason",
228	        "duration_seconds",
229	        "model",
230	        "usage",
231	        "cost_usd",
232	        "num_turns",
233	        "session_id",
234	        "is_error",
235	    }
236	    assert set(entry.keys()) == required_keys
237	    assert entry["issue_identifier"] == first["identifier"]
238	    # The agent never flipped state, so the halted entry records "Todo"
239	    # — load-bearing for KR1 grading (must distinguish halt from success).
240	    assert entry["final_linear_state"] == first["state"]["name"]
241	    assert entry["final_linear_state"] != "Done"
242	    assert entry["worktree_path"] == str(repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"])
243	
244	    # halt_reason equals the stderr halt line exactly — both
245	    # surfaces come from the same orchestrator._halt_message helper, so
246	    # the on-disk explanation cannot drift from what the operator saw at
247	    # halt time.
248	    stderr_lines = capsys.readouterr().err.splitlines()
249	    (halt_line,) = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
250	    assert entry["halt_reason"] == halt_line
251	    # And the halt line carries the identifier, state name, and absolute
252	    # worktree path (spec'd shape).
253	    assert first["identifier"] in halt_line
254	    assert first["state"]["name"] in halt_line
255	    assert str(repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"]) in halt_line
256	
257	    # Second issue never attempted: absent from log AND from disk.
258	    second_worktree = repo / ".worktrees" / second["identifier"]
259	    assert not second_worktree.exists()
260	    assert all(
261	        e["issue_identifier"] != second["identifier"] for e in payload["entries"]
262	    )
263	
264	    # Halted worktree preserved.
265	    assert (repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"]).is_dir()

268	def test_orchestrator_records_halt_when_setup_raises_before_spawn(
269	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
270	) -> None:
271	    """A pre-spawn failure (e.g. Linear outage during ``set_state``) must
272	    convert to a recorded halt — run-log entry + Halt: stderr line + exit 1
273	    — instead of an uncaught traceback. Without this, the orchestrator
274	    crashes mid-cycle and the runlog is missing the attempted entry, so
275	    KR1 grading sees a phantom-zero rather than a halt.
276	    """
277	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
278	    repo.mkdir()
279	    _init_repo(repo)
280	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
281	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
282	
283	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
284	    second = _issue("ABA-SECOND", sort_order=2.0)
285	    raw_issues = [first, second]
286	
287	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
288	        return "stub-cycle-id"
289	
290	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
291	        return linear._plan(raw_issues)
292	
293	    def failing_set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
294	        raise RuntimeError("Linear outage simulated")
295	
296	    # get_issue must never be called — the orchestrator should halt before
297	    # the spawn, so there's no post-spawn state refresh.
298	    def forbidden_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
299	        raise AssertionError("get_issue called after setup failure")
300	
301	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
302	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
303	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", failing_set_state)
304	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", forbidden_get_issue)
305	
306	    # Claude must never be spawned either — the failure is before spawn.
307	    forbidden_claude = tmp_path / "forbidden-claude.sh"
308	    forbidden_claude.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nexit 99\n")
309	    forbidden_claude.chmod(0o755)
310	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(forbidden_claude)])
311	
312	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
313	    assert exit_code == 1
314	
315	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
316	    log_files = list(runs_dir.glob("stub-cycle-id-*.json"))
317	    assert len(log_files) == 1
318	    payload = json.loads(log_files[0].read_text())
319	
320	    # One entry recorded for the failing-setup issue; second issue absent.
321	    assert len(payload["entries"]) == 1
322	    entry = payload["entries"][0]
323	    assert entry["issue_identifier"] == first["identifier"]
324	    # Final Linear state is the pre-failure state — set_state never landed.
325	    assert entry["final_linear_state"] == first["state"]["name"]
326	    # halt_reason carries the same string emitted to stderr.
327	    stderr_lines = capsys.readouterr().err.splitlines()
328	    (halt_line,) = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
329	    assert entry["halt_reason"] == halt_line
330	    # The halt message surfaces the underlying exception so the operator
331	    # can diagnose without re-reading a stack trace.
332	    assert "Linear outage simulated" in halt_line
333	    assert "setup failed" in halt_line
334	
335	    # Second issue never attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
336	    assert all(
337	        e["issue_identifier"] != second["identifier"] for e in payload["entries"]
338	    )

343	) -> None:

354	
355	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
356	    second = _issue("ABA-SECOND", sort_order=2.0)

358	
359	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:

378	    # ``subprocess.run(timeout=…)`` raises TimeoutExpired.

380	    hanging_claude.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nsleep 10\n")

391	    assert len(payload["entries"]) == 1

395

397	    (halt_line,) = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
398	    assert entry["halt_reason"] == halt_line
399	    assert "per-issue time cap exceeded" in halt_line
400	
401	    # Worktree preserved for inspection (existing halt contract).
402	    assert (repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"]).is_dir()
403	    # Second issue never attempted.
404	    assert all(
405	        e["issue_identifier"] != second["identifier"] for e in payload["entries"]
406	    )

409	def test_orchestrator_reverts_state_on_timeout_halt(
410	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
411	) -> None:
412	    """On spawn-timeout halt, the orchestrator must revert the issue's state
413	    back to the pre-halt state name captured from the cycle query — so a
414	    re-run picks it up again rather than silently skipping it (the
415	    silent-skip case).
416	    """
417	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
418	    repo.mkdir()
419	    _init_repo(repo)
420	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
421	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
422	
423	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
424	    raw_issues = [first]
425	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
426	    set_state_calls: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
427	    # Tracks Linear-side state per issue so a successful revert is visible
428	    # on the post-revert refresh.
429	    live_states: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {
430	        first["id"]: first["state"].copy(),
431	    }
432	
433	    def fake_set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
434	        set_state_calls.append((issue_id, state_name))
435	        live_states[issue_id] = {
436	            "type": "started" if state_name == "In Progress" else "unstarted",
437	            "name": state_name,
438	        }
439	
440	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
441	        return {**issues_by_id[issue_id], "state": live_states[issue_id]}
442	
443	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle-id")
444	    monkeypatch.setattr(
445	        linear, "pending_issues", lambda cycle_id: linear._plan(raw_issues)
446	    )
447	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", fake_set_state)
448	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
449	
450	    hanging_claude = tmp_path / "hanging-claude.sh"
451	    hanging_claude.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nsleep 10\n")
452	    hanging_claude.chmod(0o755)
453	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(hanging_claude)])
454	
455	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(
456	        _stub_repos(repo), limits.Limits(per_issue_seconds=0.3)
457	    )
458	    assert exit_code == 1
459	
460	    # Two set_state calls in pick order: In Progress (pre-spawn), then
461	    # revert to the original "Todo" (post-halt).
462	    assert set_state_calls == [
463	        (first["id"], "In Progress"),
464	        (first["id"], first["state"]["name"]),
465	    ]
466	    # Linear ends in the pre-halt state — what makes a re-run pick it up.
467	    assert live_states[first["id"]]["name"] == first["state"]["name"]
468	
469	    # The halt entry's final_linear_state reflects the post-revert state
470	    # observed via refresh, not the in-flight "In Progress".
471	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
472	    payload = json.loads(next(runs_dir.glob("stub-cycle-id-*.json")).read_text())
473	    entry = payload["entries"][0]
474	    assert entry["final_linear_state"] == first["state"]["name"]
475	
476	    # Same string lands on stderr (invariant).
477	    stderr_lines = capsys.readouterr().err.splitlines()
478	    (halt_line,) = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
479	    assert entry["halt_reason"] == halt_line
480	    assert first["state"]["name"] in halt_line
481

545	def test_orchestrator_halt_records_revert_failure_non_fatally(
546	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
547	) -> None:
548	    """If the revert ``set_state`` call raises, the orchestrator still exits
549	    1 with a halt entry written. The halt-reason explicitly
550	    notes the revert failure so the operator can fix the state by hand,
551	    and no traceback escapes.
552	    """
553	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
554	    repo.mkdir()
555	    _init_repo(repo)
556	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
557	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
558	
559	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
560	    raw_issues = [first]
561	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
562	    set_state_calls: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
563	    revert_error_message = "Linear unavailable during revert"
564	
565	    def fake_set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
566	        set_state_calls.append((issue_id, state_name))
567	        # First call (Todo → In Progress) succeeds; second call (revert)
568	        # raises to simulate a Linear outage at exactly the wrong moment.
569	        if len(set_state_calls) >= 2:
570	            raise RuntimeError(revert_error_message)
571	
572	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
573	        # Agent left the issue in In Progress. Revert will be attempted
574	        # but will fail.
575	        return {
576	            **issues_by_id[issue_id],
577	            "state": {"type": "started", "name": "In Progress"},
578	        }
579	
580	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle-id")
581	    monkeypatch.setattr(
582	        linear, "pending_issues", lambda cycle_id: linear._plan(raw_issues)
583	    )
584	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", fake_set_state)
585	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
586	
587	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path)
588	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
589	
590	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
591	    assert exit_code == 1
592	
593	    # Revert was attempted: In Progress (pre-spawn) and then the failing
594	    # revert call targeting the original state.
595	    assert set_state_calls == [
596	        (first["id"], "In Progress"),
597	        (first["id"], first["state"]["name"]),
598	    ]
599	
600	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
601	    payload = json.loads(next(runs_dir.glob("stub-cycle-id-*.json")).read_text())
602	    assert len(payload["entries"]) == 1
603	    entry = payload["entries"][0]
604	    assert entry["issue_identifier"] == first["identifier"]
605	
606	    # Halt-reason on stderr and in the runlog explicitly surfaces the
607	    # revert failure so the operator knows the state needs hand-fixing.
608	    stderr_lines = capsys.readouterr().err.splitlines()
609	    (halt_line,) = [line for line in stderr_lines if line.startswith("Halt: ")]
610	    assert entry["halt_reason"] == halt_line
611	    assert "revert" in halt_line.lower()
612	    assert revert_error_message in halt_line
613	    # No traceback escaped onto stderr.
614	    assert not any("Traceback" in line for line in stderr_lines)
615

617	def test_orchestrator_setup_failure_does_not_attempt_revert(
618	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
619	) -> None:
620	    """Pre-spawn setup-failure path is unchanged — when
621	    ``worktree.add`` or the initial ``linear.set_state`` itself raised,
622	    no revert is needed because the state never moved. This pins the
623	    "only one set_state call observed" invariant.
624	    """
625	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
626	    repo.mkdir()
627	    _init_repo(repo)
628	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
629	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
630	
631	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
632	    raw_issues = [first]
633	    set_state_calls: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
634	
635	    def failing_set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
636	        set_state_calls.append((issue_id, state_name))
637	        raise RuntimeError("Linear outage simulated")
638	
639	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle-id")
640	    monkeypatch.setattr(
641	        linear, "pending_issues", lambda cycle_id: linear._plan(raw_issues)
642	    )
643	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", failing_set_state)
644	    monkeypatch.setattr(
645	        linear,
646	        "get_issue",
647	        lambda issue_id: (_ for _ in ()).throw(AssertionError("get_issue called")),
648	    )
649	
650	    forbidden_claude = tmp_path / "forbidden-claude.sh"
651	    forbidden_claude.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nexit 99\n")
652	    forbidden_claude.chmod(0o755)
653	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(forbidden_claude)])
654	
655	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
656	    assert exit_code == 1
657	    # Exactly one set_state call — the failed pre-spawn In Progress
658	    # transition. No revert attempt follows, because the state never moved.
659	    assert set_state_calls == [(first["id"], "In Progress")]
660

662	def test_orchestrator_rerun_after_halt_picks_up_same_issue(
663	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
664	) -> None:
665	    """After a halted run that successfully reverts state, a
666	    second ``orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))`` invocation against the same cycle picks
667	    up the previously-halted issue first — because the revert restored
668	    it to a ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` value and the existing sort key
669	    preserves its priority position.
670	    """
671	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
672	    repo.mkdir()
673	    _init_repo(repo)
674	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
675	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
676	
677	    first = _issue("ABA-FIRST", sort_order=1.0)
678	    second = _issue("ABA-SECOND", sort_order=2.0)
679	    raw_issues = [first, second]
680	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
681	    live_states: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {
682	        i["id"]: i["state"].copy() for i in raw_issues
683	    }
684	    picked_first: list[str] = []
685	
686	    def fake_set_state(issue_id: str, state_name: str) -> None:
687	        live_states[issue_id] = {
688	            "type": "started" if state_name == "In Progress" else "unstarted",
689	            "name": state_name,
690	        }
691	
692	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str):
693	        # Mirror the real filter: only Backlog/Todo (unstarted) come back.
694	        return linear._plan(
695	            [
696	                {**issues_by_id[i["id"]], "state": live_states[i["id"]]}
697	                for i in raw_issues
698	                if live_states[i["id"]]["type"] in ("backlog", "unstarted")
699	            ]
700	        )
701	
702	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
703	        return {**issues_by_id[issue_id], "state": live_states[issue_id]}
704	
705	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle-id")
706	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
707	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", fake_set_state)
708	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
709	
710	    # Capture the first-picked identifier per run. The fake records it on
711	    # invocation (when cwd == the worktree for the picked issue), then
712	    # exits without flipping state — halting the run.
713	    trace = tmp_path / "picked.log"
714	    fake_claude = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
715	    fake_claude.write_text(
716	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
717	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{trace}"\n'
718	        "exit 0\n"
719	    )
720	    fake_claude.chmod(0o755)
721	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
722	
723	    first_run_exit = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
724	    assert first_run_exit == 1
725	    # State reverted to Todo so a re-run can rediscover it.
726	    assert live_states[first["id"]]["name"] == first["state"]["name"]
727	
728	    # Remove the halted worktree so the second run can recreate one for
729	    # the same issue (git worktree add fails if the directory exists).
730	    halted_worktree = repo / ".worktrees" / first["identifier"]
731	    if halted_worktree.is_dir():
732	        import shutil
733	        shutil.rmtree(halted_worktree)
734	    subprocess.run(
735	        ["git", "worktree", "prune"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
736	    )
737	    subprocess.run(
738	        ["git", "branch", "-D", first["identifier"]],
739	        cwd=repo,
740	        check=False,
741	        capture_output=True,
742	    )
743	
744	    second_run_exit = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))
745	    assert second_run_exit == 1
746	
747	    picked = trace.read_text().splitlines()
748	    # First pick on each run is the same identifier — the previously
749	    # halted issue, not the lower-priority second issue.
750	    assert picked[0] == first["identifier"]
751	    assert picked[1] == first["identifier"]
752

1	# Design decisions
2	
3	Design rationale for `drain-cycle`. Read this before making architectural changes — `AGENTS.md` points here.
4	
5	Thirteen decisions are recorded so a future reader doesn't have to reverse-engineer them from the code. ADRs would be heavier than this tool needs.
6	
7	## 1. The spawned agent updates Linear itself
8	
9	The orchestrator does **not** poll Linear and write status. The spawned `claude -p` session is told, in its prompt, to move its issue to Done on completion. The orchestrator only reads Linear after the session exits, to decide whether to advance or halt.
10	
11	**Alternative considered.** Orchestrator-owned status: the parent polls Linear, transitions states, owns the lifecycle. This is more conventional and easier to reason about.
12	
13	**Why the agent-self-update path.** The orchestrator can only observe *process exit*, not *task success*. A Claude session may exit 0 having done nothing useful, or exit non-zero having actually shipped — exit code is a poor proxy for "the issue is Done." Letting the agent assert Done in Linear forces it to make an explicit, observable claim about its own outcome, which is exactly the artefact we need to grade KR1 and trigger the kill condition. If this pattern proves unreliable, that's the initiative's kill condition firing — not a bug to paper over.
14	
15	## 2. `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is accepted
16	
17	Every spawned session runs with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. The agent can run any tool on any path inside its worktree, including shell commands, file writes, and network calls, with no operator approval.
18	
19	**Blast radius.** Bounded to the per-issue worktree under `.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/` inside the target repo, plus the operator's Linear account (the agent can transition issues), plus whatever the spawned shell can reach (env vars, secrets in `~/.config`, network egress). Not bounded to the worktree at the filesystem level — a determined or confused agent can `cd` out.
20	
21	**Why accepted.** The single-operator personal-product context: target repos are mine, the Linear workspace is mine, the machine is mine. The point of the tool is removing prompts; gating them defeats the purpose. The mitigation is **scope discipline at cycle planning** — don't drain a cycle whose issues touch credentials, production systems, or shared infrastructure. This is operator responsibility, not a tool guarantee.
22	
23	## 3. Fresh worktree per issue, not a shared workspace
24	
25	Each issue gets `.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/` branched off `main`, used once, then either removed (on Done) or preserved (on halt).
26	
27	**Alternative considered.** Shared workspace where all issues run in the target repo's main checkout in sequence. Simpler, faster, no worktree plumbing.
28	
29	**Why worktree-per-issue.** Issues drift. An agent that misunderstands its task can leave the workspace in a broken state — half-applied edits, uncommitted files, branch in the wrong place — that contaminates every subsequent issue's starting point. The worktree gives each issue a clean, identical starting point regardless of what the previous one did, and preservation-on-halt (US-B) means inspectable debug state. The cost is filesystem space and a few seconds of branch setup per issue. Cheap.
30	
31	## 4. Run-log is one file per invocation, not one file per cycle
32	
33	Each `drain-cycle` invocation writes its own run-log file at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. The per-file schema is unchanged — `{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds, entries: [...]}` — and `cycle_id` inside each file is how downstream readers group runs of the same cycle.
34	
35	**Alternatives considered.** (A) Multi-run schema in one file: `{cycle_id, runs: [...]}`. Faithful, but every reader has to learn the new shape and the on-disk backup file needs migrating. (B) Open-and-extend: load the existing file and append to a single flat `entries` list. Loses run boundaries, and `cycle_duration_seconds` (computed as `max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`) spans the inter-run gap and becomes misleading. (D) Refuse to clobber: fail-fast if the file exists. Breaks the unattended re-run flow the tool exists for (fix X, re-run, drain the rest) until a resume mode is built.
36	
37	**Why per-run files.** The bug being fixed (ABA-230) is that a second invocation against the same cycle silently overwrites the first run's data. Per-run files make the write path single-writer-write-once — no read-then-write race, no schema diff, no migration. US-D (ABA-197), which already plans to glob `runs/*.json` and merge across cycles, gets a one-line addition (group by `cycle_id`) instead of a new shape to read. Each file's `cycle_duration_seconds` represents one invocation's hands-off time; US-D sums them when reporting cycle-level KR2.
38	
39	**Cost.** A re-run cycle accumulates one file per invocation. At cycle scale (≤ 15 issues, rarely more than 2–3 invocations to drain) this is negligible. No retention policy is shipped; trim by hand if it ever matters.
40	
41	## 5. Each issue declares its target repo via a `repo:<name>` Linear label
42	
43	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: `repo = Path.cwd()`, every worktree under `<cwd>/.worktrees/`. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos by design — `linear-workflow.md` makes "Affected repos" part of the six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds cross-repo maintenance issues. Each issue now carries a `repo:<name>` Linear label; `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml` maps the name to an absolute path; the operator runs `drain-cycle` from anywhere.
44	
45	**Grouped labels are supported.** The Linear workspace uses label groups (`repo`, `model`, `wave`, …). A child label in the `repo` group — e.g. the leaf `drain-cycle` under the `repo` group — is indistinguishable from a flat `repo:drain-cycle` label to `repos.resolve`. The `pending_issues` query fetches `labels { nodes { name parent { name } } }` and renders each grouped node as `"<group>:<name>"` via `_label_name`; ungrouped nodes keep their bare name (backward-compatible with any literal `repo:<name>` labels already in use). The same rendering applies to `model` group children for `model.resolve` (§7).
46	
47	**Alternatives considered.**
48	
49	- *Description-body encoding* (e.g. a `Repo: drain-cycle` line in the markdown). The description is the most-edited surface, agents rewrite it routinely, and there's no schema enforcement. A label is one structured field with one value — Linear validates it, and a label rename triggers a clear "this label doesn't exist" error rather than silently drifting to a wrong repo.
50	- *Title prefix* (e.g. `[drain-cycle] Fix the foo`). Cleaner machine parse than the body, but every issue's title gains visual clutter for a parser-only concern. Labels don't pay that cost.
51	- *Inherit from the Linear project's "affected repos"*. Ambiguous for projects that legitimately touch multiple repos, and there is no obvious answer for the Ops container project, which is multi-repo by definition.
52	
53	**Missing-repo halt behaviour.** Same machinery as every other pre-spawn halt: write a run-log entry, print the `Halt:` line to stderr, exit 1, leave subsequent issues untouched. The new wrinkle is that resolution failures happen before any Linear state is moved, so they skip the post-spawn revert path (`_revert_to_pre_halt_state`). This is the difference from the existing setup-failure halt: that one happens after `worktree.add` or the initial `linear.set_state` attempt; the resolution halt happens before either. Either way, no revert is needed because no state was moved.
54	
55	**`repos.yml` config errors are even earlier.** A missing or malformed `repos.yml` halts the CLI at startup, before any Linear traffic and before the run-log file is created. There is no cycle yet to log against, so the failure surfaces only on stderr. This is enforced eagerly in `cli.main` so the orchestrator never sees a broken config.
56	
57	**Why labels over file conventions.** (e.g. requiring the operator to put the issue identifier in a branch comment, or use a remote-name convention). Labels are the only signal that's enforceable in Linear's UI: cycle planners can see at a glance which repo an issue targets, and the multi-repo distinction is visible at the right surface (Linear) rather than buried in a config file.
58	
59	**Out-of-v1 deliberately.** No env-var expansion inside `repos.yml`; no auto-clone if the path is missing; no parallelism across repos; no retroactive labelling of pre-cycle issues. All are operator-time concerns rather than tool-time concerns. Multi-team Linear support stays out of scope too — the tool is still hardcoded to the `Personal` team.
60	
61	## 6. Installed as a `uv tool`, with the secret read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env`
62	
63	`drain-cycle` is installed via `uv tool install`, which puts the executable on `$PATH` in an isolated environment. The Linear API key is read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env` (shell-exported vars still win), beside the `repos.yml` config and the `runs/` logs the tool already kept there.
64	
65	**Alternatives considered.**
66	
67	- *`pipx`*. Functionally equivalent for installing a Python CLI in isolation. Rejected because `uv` already anchors this repo's stack (`uv.lock`, the `mise.toml` Python pin) — adding `pipx` spends an innovation token on a second tool that does the same job.
68	- *Publish to PyPI*. Lets anyone `uv tool install drain-cycle` by name, but buys a release-and-versioning burden — tagging, changelogs, a name on the index — that a single-operator tool doesn't earn. `uv tool install git+https://…` already covers install-from-anywhere with no release step.
69	
70	**The secret-loading change this forced.** The CLI used to load `.env` only from the repo root (`Path(__file__).parent.parent`). Once installed, the package lives in the isolated tool env, where that path has no `.env` — so the key has to live somewhere stable. The load order is now shell env → `~/.drain-cycle/.env` → repo-root `.env`, first hit wins (`load_dotenv` defaults to `override=False`). The repo-root entry survives only as a dev-checkout fallback (it works under `--editable`, where `__file__` still points into the checkout); the installed tool reads the key from `~/.drain-cycle/.env`.
71	
72	**Out-of-v1 deliberately.** No PyPI release. No `drain-cycle init` command to scaffold `~/.drain-cycle/` — a missing `repos.yml` already halts with an actionable message that prints the expected shape, and `docs/repos.example.yml` is a copyable template, which is enough for a single operator.
73	
74	## 7. Workers default to Sonnet; a `model:` label overrides per issue
75	
76	A spawned `claude -p` worker inherits whatever model the operator has globally pinned. In the diagnosed quota-burn run all five workers ran on `claude-opus-4-7` (the operator's global pin), and Opus was the single largest cost multiplier of the ~108M-token spend. Workers now default to `claude-sonnet-4-6`, passed explicitly via `--model`; an individual issue opts up (or down) with a `model:<alias>` Linear label, mirroring the `repo:<name>` mechanism. Known aliases (`sonnet`/`opus`/`haiku`) map to full ids; an unrecognised value is passed to `claude --model` verbatim.
77	
78	**Why lenient, not strict.** Unlike `repo:` resolution — where a missing label is a hard halt because there is no safe default target — model resolution always has a safe fallback. So it never raises: no label, an unknown alias, or conflicting `model:` labels all fall back to the default rather than halting an unattended cycle over a label typo. The model actually used is recorded in the run log, so a mis-labelled issue surfaces after the fact instead of stalling the run.
79	
80	**Grouped model labels.** A child of the Linear `model` group — e.g. the leaf `sonnet` under `model` — renders as `model:sonnet` via the same `_label_name` projection described in §5. `model.resolve` sees it identically to a flat `model:sonnet` label.
```
